**34** But the Pharisees kept saying, "He drives out demons by the ruler of demons." <sup>&#123;Other mss. lack this verse&#125;</sup>

**35** <sup>&#123;The Compassion of Jesus&#125;</sup> d Then Jesus began traveling throughout all the cities and villages, teaching in their synagogues, proclaiming the gospel of the kingdom, and healing every disease and every illness.

**36** When he saw the crowds, he was deeply moved with compassion for them, because they were troubled and helpless, like sheep without a shepherd.

**37** Then he told his disciples, "The harvest is vast, but the workers are few.

**38** So ask the Lord of the harvest to send out workers into his harvest."

## Chapter 10

**1** <sup>&#123;Jesus Appoints Twelve Apostles&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 3:13-19; Luke 6:12-16)&#125;</sup> d Then Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. he&#125;</sup> called his twelve disciples to him and gave them authority over unclean spirits, so that they could drive them out and heal every disease and every illness.

**2** These are the names of the twelve apostles: first, Simon (who is called Peter) and his brother Andrew; James, the son of Zebedee, and his brother John;

**3** Philip and Bartholomew; Thomas and Matthew the tax collector; James, the son of Alphaeus, and Thaddaeus; <sup>&#123;Other mss. read Lebbaeus called Thaddaeus&#125;</sup>

**4** Simon the Cananaean <sup>&#123;Cananaean is Aram. for Zealot .&#125;</sup> and Judas Iscariot, who later <sup>&#123;Lit. also&#125;</sup> betrayed Jesus. <sup>&#123;Lit. him&#125;</sup>

**5** <sup>&#123;Jesus Sends Out the Twelve Disciples&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 6:7-13; Luke 9:1-6)&#125;</sup> d These were the Twelve whom Jesus sent out after he had given them these <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks these&#125;</sup> instructions: "Don't turn on to the road that leads to the unbelievers, <sup>&#123;Lit. gentiles; i.e. unbelieving non-Jews&#125;</sup> and don't enter Samaritan towns.

**6** Instead, go to the lost sheep of the nation <sup>&#123;Lit. house&#125;</sup> of Israel.

**7** As you go, make this announcement: «The kingdom from <sup>&#123;Lit. of&#125;</sup> heaven is near!»

**8** Heal the sick, raise the dead, cleanse lepers, drive out demons. You have received without payment, so give without payment.

**9** Don't take any gold, silver, or copper in your moneybags,

**10** or a traveling bag for the trip, or an extra shirt, <sup>&#123;Lit. two shirts&#125;</sup> or sandals, or a walking stick, because a worker deserves his food.

**11** "Whatever town or village you enter, find out who is receptive <sup>&#123;Lit. worthy&#125;</sup> in it and stay there until you leave.

**12** As you enter the house, greet its occupants.  <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks its occupants&#125;</sup>

**13** If the household is receptive, <sup>&#123;Lit. worthy&#125;</sup> let your blessing of peace come on it. But if it isn't receptive, <sup>&#123;Lit. worthy&#125;</sup> let your blessing of peace return to you.

**14** If no one welcomes you or listens to your words, as you leave that house or town, shake its dust off your feet.

**15** I tell all of you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> with certainty, it will be more bearable for the region of Sodom and Gomorrah on the day of judgment than for that town!"

**16** <sup>&#123;Future Persecutions&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Matthew 24:9-14; Mark 13:9-13; Luke 21:12-19)&#125;</sup> d "See, I am sending you out like sheep among wolves. So be as cunning as serpents and as innocent as doves.

**17** Watch out for people who will hand you over to the local councils and whip you in their synagogues.

**18** You will be brought before governors and kings because of me, to testify to them and to unbelievers.  <sup>&#123;Lit. gentiles ; i.e. unbelieving non-Jews&#125;</sup>

**19** When they hand you over, don't worry about how you are to speak <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks you are to speak&#125;</sup> or what you are to say, because in that hour what you are to say will be given to you.

**20** It won't be you speaking, but the Spirit of your Father speaking through <sup>&#123;Or in&#125;</sup> you.

**21** "Brother will hand brother over for execution, and a father his child. Children will rebel against parents and have them put to death.

**22** You will be hated by everyone because of my name. But the person who endures to the end will be saved.

**23** So when they persecute you in one town, flee to the next, because I tell all of you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> with certainty that you will not have gone through the towns of Israel before the Son of Man comes.

**24** "A disciple is not above his teacher, and a slave is not above his master.

**25** It is enough for a disciple to be like his teacher and a slave to be like his master. If they have called the head of the house Beelzebul, <sup>&#123;The name means Lord of the Flies ; i.e. Satan&#125;</sup> how much more will they do the same to <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks will they do the same to&#125;</sup> those of his household!"

**26** <sup>&#123;Fear God&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Luke 12:2-7)&#125;</sup> d "So never be afraid of them, because there is nothing hidden that will not be revealed, and nothing secret that will not be made known.

**27** What I tell you in darkness you must speak in the daylight, and what is whispered <sup>&#123;Lit. what you hear&#125;</sup> in your ear you must shout from the housetops.

**28** Stop being  <sup>&#123;Or Don't be&#125;</sup> afraid of those who kill the body but can't kill the soul. Instead, be afraid of the one who can destroy both body and soul in hell. <sup>&#123;Gehenna ; a Gk. transliteration of the Heb. for Valley of Hinnom&#125;</sup>

**29** "Two sparrows are sold for a penny, aren't they? Yet not one of them will fall to the ground without your Father's permission.  <sup>&#123;Lit. apart from your Father&#125;</sup>

**30** Indeed, even the hairs on your head have all been counted!

**31** So stop being <sup>&#123;Or don't be&#125;</sup> afraid. You are worth more than a bunch of sparrows."

**32** <sup>&#123;Acknowledging the Messiah&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Luke 12:8-9)&#125;</sup> d "Therefore, everyone who acknowledges me before people I, too, will acknowledge before my Father in heaven.

**33** But whoever denies me before people I, too, will deny before my Father in heaven."

**34** <sup>&#123;Not Peace, but Division&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Luke 12:49-53)&#125;</sup> d "Do not think that I came to bring peace on earth. I did not come to bring peace but a sword! <sup>&#123;I.e. conflict&#125;</sup>

**35** I came to turn «a man against his father, a daughter against her mother, and a daughter-in-law against her mother-in-law.

**36** A person's enemies will include members of his own family.' <sup>&#123;Mic 7:6&#125;</sup>

**37** <sup>&#123;The Cost of Discipleship&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Luke 14:25-27)&#125;</sup> d "The one who loves his father or mother more than me isn't worthy of me, and the one who loves a son or daughter more than me isn't worthy of me.

**38** The one who doesn't take up his cross and follow me isn't worthy of me.

**39** The one who finds his life will lose it, and the one who loses his life because of me will find it."

**40** <sup>&#123;Rewards&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 9:41)&#125;</sup> d "The one who receives you receives me, and the one who receives me receives the one who sent me.

**41** The one who receives a prophet as <sup>&#123;Lit. in the name of&#125;</sup> a prophet will receive a prophet's reward, and the one who receives a righteous person as <sup>&#123;Lit. in the name of&#125;</sup> a righteous person will receive a righteous person's reward.

**42** I tell all of you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> with certainty, whoever gives even a cup of cold water to one of these little ones because he is <sup>&#123;Lit. in the name of&#125;</sup> a disciple will never lose his reward."

## Chapter 11

**1** When Jesus had finished instructing his twelve disciples, he left there to teach and preach in their home towns.

**2** <sup>&#123;John the Baptist Sends Messengers to Jesus&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Luke 7:18-35)&#125;</sup> d Now when John in prison heard about the activities of the Messiah, <sup>&#123;Or Christ&#125;</sup> he sent a message <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks a message&#125;</sup> by his disciples

**3** and asked him, "Are you the Coming One, or should we wait for someone else?"

**4** Jesus answered them, "Go and tell John what you hear and observe:

**5** the blind see, the lame walk, lepers are cleansed, the deaf hear, the dead are raised, and the destitute hear the good news.

**6** How blessed is anyone who is not offended by me!"

**7** As they were leaving, Jesus began to speak to the crowds about John. "What did you go out into the wilderness to see? A reed shaken by the wind?

**8** Really, what did you go out to see? A man dressed in fancy clothes? See, those who wear fancy clothes live in kings'houses.

**9** Really, what did you go out to see? A prophet? Yes, I tell you, and even more than a prophet!

**10** This is the man about whom it is written, «See, I am sending my messenger ahead of you, who will prepare your way before you.' <sup>&#123;Mal 3:1&#125;</sup>

**11** I tell all of you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> with certainty, among those born of women no one has appeared who is greater than John the Baptist. Yet even the least important person in the kingdom from <sup>&#123;Lit. of&#125;</sup> heaven is greater than he.

**12** "From the days of John the Baptist until the present, the kingdom from <sup>&#123;Lit. of&#125;</sup> heaven has been forcefully advancing, and violent people have been attacking it,

**13** because the Law and all the Prophets prophesied up to the time of John.

**14** If you are willing to accept it, he is Elijah who is to come.  <sup>&#123;Or is about to come&#125;</sup>

**15** Let the person who has ears <sup>&#123;Other mss. read ears to hear&#125;</sup> listen!

**16** "To what can I compare the people living today? They're <sup>&#123;Lit. compare this generation? It's&#125;</sup> like little children who sit in the marketplaces and shout to each other,

**17** «A wedding song we played for you, the dance you simply scorned. A woeful dirge we chanted, too, but then you would not mourn.»

**18** Because John didn't come eating or drinking, yet people <sup>&#123;Lit. they&#125;</sup> say, «He has a demon!»

**19** The Son of Man came eating and drinking, and they say, «Look, a glutton and a drunk, a friend of tax collectors and sinners!» Absolved from every act of sin, is wisdom by her kith and kin."  <sup>&#123;Lit. by all her children; other mss. read by her actions&#125;</sup>

**20** <sup>&#123;Jesus Denounces Unrepentant Cities&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Luke 10:13-15)&#125;</sup> d Then Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. he&#125;</sup> began to denounce the cities in which most of his miracles had taken place, because they didn't repent.

**21** "How terrible it will be for you, Chorazin! How terrible it will be for you, Bethsaida! Because if the miracles that happened in you had taken place in Tyre and Sidon, they would have repented long ago in sackcloth and ashes.

**22** Indeed I tell you, it will be more bearable for Tyre and Sidon on the day of judgment than for you!

**23** "And you, Capernaum! You won't be lifted up to heaven, will you? You'll go down to Hell! <sup>&#123;Lit. Hades ; i.e. the realm of the dead&#125;</sup> Because if the miracles that happened in you had taken place in Sodom, it would have remained to this day.

**24** Indeed I tell you, it will be more bearable for the land of Sodom on the day of judgment than for you!"

**25** <sup>&#123;Jesus Praises the Father and Invites the Disciples to Come to Him&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Luke 10:21-22)&#125;</sup> d At that time Jesus said, "I praise you, Father, Lord of heaven and earth, because you have hidden these things from wise and intelligent people and have revealed them to infants.

**26** Yes, Father, because this is what was pleasing to you.

**27** All things have been entrusted to me by my Father. No one fully knows the Son except the Father, and no one fully knows the Father except the Son and the person to whom the Son chooses to reveal him.

**28** "Come to me, all of you who are weary and loaded down with burdens, and I will give you rest.

**29** Place my yoke on you and learn from me, because I am gentle and humble,  <sup>&#123;Lit. humble in heart&#125;</sup> and you will find rest for your souls, <sup>&#123;cf. Jer 6:16&#125;</sup>

**30** because my yoke is pleasant, <sup>&#123;Or kind&#125;</sup> and my burden is light."

## Chapter 12

**1** <sup>&#123;Jesus is Lord of the Sabbath&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 2:23-28; Luke 6:1-5)&#125;</sup> d At that time Jesus walked through the grain fields on a Sabbath. <sup>&#123;Lit. on the Sabbaths&#125;</sup> His disciples became hungry and began picking heads of grain to eat.

**2** When the Pharisees saw this, they told him, "Look! Your disciples are doing what is not lawful to do on the Sabbath!"

**3** But he told them, "Haven't you read what David did when he and his companions were hungry?

**4** How is it that he went into the house of God and ate the Bread of the Presence, which was not lawful for him and his companions to eat but was reserved <sup>&#123;Lit. but only&#125;</sup> for the priests?

**5** Or haven't you read in the Law that on every Sabbath  <sup>&#123;Lit. on the Sabbaths&#125;</sup> the priests in the Temple violate the Sabbath <sup>&#123;I.e. by carrying out priestly duties&#125;</sup> and yet are innocent? <sup>&#123;Exod 25:30; 29:32-33; Lev 8:31&#125;</sup>

**6** But I tell you, something greater than the Temple is here!

**7** If you had known what «I want mercy and not sacrifice'<sup>&#123;Hos 6:6&#125;</sup> means, you would not have condemned the innocent,

**8** for the Son of Man is Lord of the Sabbath."

**9** <sup>&#123;Jesus Heals a Man with a Paralyzed Hand&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 3:1-6; Luke 6:6-11)&#125;</sup> d Moving on from there, Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. he&#125;</sup> went into their synagogue.

**10** Suddenly, a man with a paralyzed hand appeared. The people <sup>&#123;Lit. They&#125;</sup> asked Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. him&#125;</sup> if it was lawful to heal on Sabbath days, <sup>&#123;Lit. on the Sabbaths&#125;</sup> intending to accuse him of doing something wrong.

**11** But he asked them, "Is there a man among you who, if he had one sheep and it fell into a ditch on the Sabbath, wouldn't take hold of it and pull it out?

**12** How much more is a human being worth than a sheep! So it is lawful to do good on Sabbath days."  <sup>&#123;Lit. on the Sabbaths&#125;</sup>

**13** Then he told the man, "Hold out your hand." He held it out and it became normal, as healthy as his other hand.

**14** The Pharisees, however, went out and plotted against Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. him&#125;</sup> to kill him.

**15** <sup>&#123;Jesus, God's Chosen Servant&#125;</sup> d When Jesus became aware of this, he left that place. Many crowds <sup>&#123;Other mss. lack crowds&#125;</sup> followed him, and he healed all of them,

**16** ordering them not to make him known.

**17** This was to fulfill what was declared by the prophet Isaiah when he said,

**18** "Here is my Servant whom I have chosen, whom I love, and with whom I am pleased! I will put my Spirit on him, and he will proclaim justice <sup>&#123;Or judgment&#125;</sup> to unbelievers. <sup>&#123;Lit. gentiles ; i.e. unbelieving non-Jews&#125;</sup>

**19** He will not quarrel or shout, and no one will hear him shouting <sup>&#123;Lit. hear his voice&#125;</sup> in the streets.

**20** He will not snap off a broken reed or snuff out a smoldering wick until he has brought justice <sup>&#123;Or judgment&#125;</sup> through to victory.

**21** And in his name unbelievers <sup>&#123;Lit. gentiles ; i.e. unbelieving non-Jews&#125;</sup> will hope." <sup>&#123;Isa 42:1-4&#125;</sup>

**22** <sup>&#123;Jesus is Accused of Working with Beelzebul&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 3:20-30; Luke 11:14-23; 12:10)&#125;</sup> d Then a demon-possessed man who was blind and unable to talk was brought to him. Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. He&#125;</sup> healed him so that the man <sup>&#123;Lit. the man who was unable to talk&#125;</sup> could speak and see.

**23** All the crowds were amazed and kept saying, "This man isn't the Son of David, is he?"

**24** But when the Pharisees heard this, they said, "This man drives out demons only by Beelzebul, the ruler of the demons."

**25** He knew what they were thinking and told them, "Every kingdom divided against itself is destroyed, and every city or household divided against itself will not stand.

**26** So if Satan drives out Satan, he is divided against himself. How, then, can his kingdom stand?

**27** If I drive out demons by Beelzebul, by whom do your own followers <sup>&#123;Lit. sons&#125;</sup> drive them out? That is why they will be your judges!

**28** But if I drive out demons by the Spirit of God, then the kingdom of God has come to you.

**29** How can someone go into a strong man's house and carry off his possessions without first tying up the strong man? Then he can ransack his house.

**30** "The person who isn't with me is against me, and the person who isn't gathering with me is scattering.

**31** So I tell you, every sin and blasphemy will be forgiven, <sup>&#123;Lit. will be forgiven to people&#125;</sup> but blasphemy against the Spirit will not be forgiven.

**32** Whoever speaks a word against the Son of Man will be forgiven, but whoever speaks against the Holy Spirit will not be forgiven, either in this age or in the one to come."

**33** <sup>&#123;A Tree is Known by Its Fruit&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Luke 6:43-45)&#125;</sup> d "Either make the tree good and its fruit good, or make the tree rotten and its fruit rotten, because a tree is known by its fruit.

**34** You children of serpents! How can you say anything good when you are evil? The mouth speaks about what overflows from the heart.

**35** A good person brings good things out of a good treasure house, and an evil person brings evil things out of an evil treasure house.

**36** I tell you, on the day of judgment people will give an account for every thoughtless <sup>&#123;Or worthless&#125;</sup> word they have uttered,

**37** because by your words you will be acquitted, and by your words you will be condemned."

**38** <sup>&#123;The Sign of Jonah&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 8:11-12; Luke 11:29-32)&#125;</sup> d Then some of the scribes and Pharisees told Jesus, <sup>&#123;Lit. him&#125;</sup> "Teacher, we want to see a sign from you."

**39** But he replied to them, "An evil and adulterous generation craves a sign. Yet no sign will be given to it except the sign of the prophet Jonah,

**40** because just as Jonah was in the stomach of the sea creature for three days and three nights, <sup>&#123;Jonah 1:17&#125;</sup> so the Son of Man will be in the heart of the earth for three days and three nights.

**41** The men of Nineveh will stand up at the judgment and condemn the people living today, <sup>&#123;Lit. judgment with this generation and condemn it&#125;</sup> because they repented at the preaching of Jonah. But look something greater than Jonah is here!

**42** The queen of the south will stand up and condemn the people living today, <sup>&#123;Lit. judgment with this generation and condemn it&#125;</sup> because she came from so far away <sup>&#123;Lit. from the ends of the earth&#125;</sup> to hear the wisdom of Solomon. But look! Something greater than Solomon is here!"

**43** <sup>&#123;The Return of the Unclean Spirit&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Luke 11:24-26)&#125;</sup> d "Whenever an unclean spirit goes out of a person, it wanders through waterless places looking for a place to rest but finds none.

**44** Then it says, «I will go back to my home that I left.'When it arrives, it finds it empty, swept clean, and put in order.

**45** Then it goes and brings with it seven other spirits more evil than itself, and they go in and settle there. And so the final condition of that person becomes worse than the first. That's just what will happen to this evil generation!"

**46** <sup>&#123;The True Family of Jesus&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 3:31-35; Luke 8:19-21)&#125;</sup> d While Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. he&#125;</sup> was still speaking to the crowds, his mother and brothers stood outside, wanting to speak to him.

**47** Someone told him, "Look! Your mother and your brothers are standing outside asking to speak to you." <sup>&#123;Other mss. lack this verse&#125;</sup>

**48** He asked the man who told him, "Who is my mother, and who are my brothers?"

**49** Then pointing with his hand at his disciples, he said, "Here are my mother and my brothers,

**50** because whoever does the will of my Father in heaven is my brother and sister and mother."

## Chapter 13

**1** <sup>&#123;The Parable about a Farmer&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 4:1-9; Luke 8:4-18)&#125;</sup> d That day Jesus left the house and sat down beside the sea.

**2** Such large crowds gathered around him that he got into a boat and sat down, while the entire crowd stood on the shore.

**3** Then he began to tell them many things in parables. He said, "Listen! A farmer went out to sow.

**4** As he was sowing, some seeds fell along the path, and birds came and ate them up.

**5** Other seeds fell on stony ground, where they did not have a lot of soil. They sprouted at once because the soil wasn't deep.

**6** But when the sun came up, they were scorched. Since they did not have any roots, they dried up.

**7** Other seeds fell among thorn bushes, and the thorn bushes grew higher and choked them out.

**8** But other seeds fell on good soil and produced a crop, some 100, some 60, and some 30 times what was sown.  <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks what was sown&#125;</sup>

**9** Let the person who has ears <sup>&#123;Other mss. read ears to hear&#125;</sup> listen!"

**10** <sup>&#123;The Purpose of the Parables&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 4:10-12; Luke 8:9-10)&#125;</sup> d Then the disciples came and asked Jesus, <sup>&#123;Lit. him&#125;</sup> "Why do you speak to people <sup>&#123;Lit. to them&#125;</sup> in parables?"

**11** He answered them, "You have been given knowledge about the secrets of the kingdom from <sup>&#123;Lit. of&#125;</sup> heaven, but it hasn't been given to them,

**12** because to anyone who has something, more will be given, and he will have more than enough. But from the one who doesn't have anything, even what he has will be taken away from him.

**13** That's why I speak to them in parables, because «they look but don't see, and they listen but don't hear or understand.»

**14** "With them the prophecy of Isaiah is being fulfilled, which says: «You will listen and listen but never understand. You will look and look but never comprehend,

**15** for this people's heart has become dull, and their ears are hard of hearing. <sup>&#123;Lit. they hear with ears of heaviness&#125;</sup> They have shut their eyes so that they might not see with their eyes, and hear with their ears, and understand with their heart and turn, and I would heal them.' <sup>&#123;Isa 6:9-10&#125;</sup>

**16** "How blessed are your eyes because they see, and your ears because they hear!

**17** I tell all of you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> with certainty, many prophets and righteous people longed to see the things you see but did not see them, and to hear the things you hear but did not hear them."

**18** <sup>&#123;Jesus Explains the Parable about the Farmer&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 4:13-20; Luke 8:11-15)&#125;</sup> d "Listen, then, to the parable about the farmer.

**19** When anyone hears the word about the kingdom yet doesn't understand it, the evil one comes and snatches away what was sown in his heart. This is what was sown along the path.

**20** As for what was sown on the stony ground, this is the person who hears the word and accepts it joyfully at once,

**21** but since he doesn't have any root in himself, he lasts for only a short time. When trouble or persecution comes along because of the word, he immediately falls away.

**22** As for what was sown among the thorn bushes, this is the person who hears the word, but the worries of life and the deceitful pleasures of wealth choke the word so that it can't produce a crop.

**23** But as for what was sown on good soil, this is the person who hears the word, understands it, and produces a crop that yields 100, 60, or 30 times what was sown."  <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks what was sown&#125;</sup>

**24** <sup>&#123;The Parable about the Weeds among the Wheat&#125;</sup> d He presented another parable to them, saying, "The kingdom from <sup>&#123;Lit. of&#125;</sup> heaven may be compared to a man who sowed good seed in his field.

**25** While people were sleeping, his enemy came and sowed weeds among the wheat and went away.

**26** When the crop came up and bore grain, the weeds appeared, too.

**27** The owner's servants came and asked him, «Master, you sowed good seed in your field, didn't you? Then where did these weeds come from?»

**28** He told them, «An enemy did this!'The servants asked him, «Then do you want us to go and pull them out?»

**29** He said, «No! If you pull out the weeds, you might pull out the wheat with them.

**30** Let both grow together until the harvest, and at harvest time I will tell the reapers, "Gather the weeds first and tie them in bundles for burning, but bring the wheat into my barn."»"

**31** <sup>&#123;The Parables about a Mustard Seed and Yeast&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 4:30-32; Luke 13:18-21)&#125;</sup> d He presented another parable to them, saying, "The kingdom from <sup>&#123;Lit. of&#125;</sup> heaven is like a mustard seed that a man took and planted in his field.

**32** Although it is the smallest of <sup>&#123;Or it is smaller than&#125;</sup> all seeds, when it is fully grown it is larger than the garden plants and becomes a tree, and the birds in the sky come and nest in its branches."

**33** He told them another parable: "The kingdom from <sup>&#123;Lit. of&#125;</sup> heaven is like yeast that a woman took and mixed with <sup>&#123;Lit. hid in&#125;</sup> three measures of flour until all of it was leavened."

**34** <sup>&#123;Why Jesus Used Parables&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 4:33-34)&#125;</sup> d Jesus told the crowds all these things in parables. He did not tell them anything without using <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks using&#125;</sup> a parable.

**35** This was to fulfill what was declared by the prophet <sup>&#123;Other mss. read Isaiah the prophet&#125;</sup> when he said, "I will open my mouth to speak <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks to speak&#125;</sup> in parables. I will declare what has been hidden since the creation of the world." <sup>&#123;Ps 78:2&#125;</sup>

**36** <sup>&#123;Jesus Explains the Parable about the Weeds&#125;</sup> d Then Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. he&#125;</sup> left the crowds and went into the house. His disciples came to him and asked, "Explain to us the parable about the weeds in the field."

**37** He answered, "The person who sowed good seed is the Son of Man,

**38** while the field is the world. The good seed are those who belong to <sup>&#123;Lit. the sons of&#125;</sup> the kingdom, while the weeds are those who belong to <sup>&#123;Lit. the sons of&#125;</sup> the evil one.

**39** The enemy who sowed them is the devil, the harvest is the end of the age, and the reapers are the angels.

**40** Just as weeds are gathered and burned with fire, so it will be at end of the <sup>&#123;Other mss. read this&#125;</sup> age.

**41** The Son of Man will send his angels, and they will gather from his kingdom everything that causes others to sin and those who practice lawlessness

**42** and they will throw them into a blazing furnace. In that place there will be wailing and gnashing of teeth.  <sup>&#123;I.e. extreme pain&#125;</sup>

**43** Then the righteous will shine like the sun in their Father's kingdom. Let the person who has ears <sup>&#123;Other mss. read ears to hear&#125;</sup> listen!"

**44** <sup>&#123;The Parable about a Hidden Treasure&#125;</sup> d "The kingdom from <sup>&#123;Lit. of&#125;</sup> heaven is like treasure hidden in a field that a man found and hid. In his excitement he went and sold everything he had and bought that field."

**45** <sup>&#123;The Parable about a Valuable Pearl&#125;</sup> d "Again, the kingdom from <sup>&#123;Lit. of&#125;</sup> heaven is like a merchant searching for fine pearls.

**46** When he found a very valuable pearl, he went and sold everything he had and bought it."

**47** <sup>&#123;The Parable about a Net&#125;</sup> d "Again, the kingdom from <sup>&#123;Lit. of&#125;</sup> heaven is like a large net thrown into the sea that gathered all kinds of fish.

**48** When it was full, the fishermen <sup>&#123;Lit. they&#125;</sup> hauled it ashore. Then they sat down, sorted the good fish into containers, and threw the bad ones away.

**49** That is how it will be at the end of the age. The angels will go out, cull out the evil people from among the righteous ones,

**50** and will throw them into a blazing furnace. In that place there will be wailing and gnashing of teeth."  <sup>&#123;I.e. extreme pain&#125;</sup>

**51** <sup>&#123;New and Old Treasures&#125;</sup> d "Do you understand all these things?" They told him, "Yes."

**52** Then he told them, "That is why every scribe who has been trained for the kingdom from <sup>&#123;Lit. of&#125;</sup> heaven is like the master of a household who brings both new and old things out of his treasure chest."

**53** <sup>&#123;Jesus is Rejected at Nazareth&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 6:1-6; Luke 4:16-30)&#125;</sup> d When Jesus had finished these parables, he left that place.

**54** He went to his hometown and began teaching the people <sup>&#123;Lit. them&#125;</sup> in their synagogue in such a way that they were amazed and asked, "Where did this man get this wisdom and these miracles?

**55** This is the builder's <sup>&#123;Or carpenter's&#125;</sup> son, isn't it? His mother is named Mary, isn't she? His brothers are James, Joseph, Simon, and Judas, aren't they?

**56** And his sisters are all with us, aren't they? So where did this man get all these things?"

**57** And they were offended by him. But Jesus told them, "A prophet is without honor only in his hometown and in his own home."

**58** He did not perform many miracles there because of their unbelief.

## Chapter 14

**1** <sup>&#123;The Death of John the Baptist&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 6:14-29; Luke 9:7-9)&#125;</sup> d At that time Herod the tetrarch, <sup>&#123;I.e. Herod Antipas, a son of King Herod and ruler over one of four districts in and around the territory of Israel.&#125;</sup> hearing about the fame of Jesus,

**2** told his servants, "This is John the Baptist! He has been raised from the dead, and that's why these miracles are being done by him."

**3** Herod had arrested John, bound him with chains, and put him in prison on account of Herodias, his brother Philip's <sup>&#123;Other mss. read his brother's&#125;</sup> wife.

**4** John had been telling him, "It is not lawful for you to have her."

**5** Although Herod <sup>&#123;Lit. he&#125;</sup> wanted to kill him, he was afraid of the crowd, since they regarded John <sup>&#123;Lit. him&#125;</sup> as a prophet.

**6** But when Herod's birthday celebration was held, the daughter of Herodias danced before the guests. <sup>&#123;Lit. in the middle&#125;</sup> She pleased Herod

**7** so much that he promised with an oath to give her whatever she asked for.

**8** Prompted by her mother, she said, "Give me, right here on a platter, the head of John the Baptist."

**9** Under pressure because of his promises and his assembled guests, the king ordered that it be done.

**10** So he sent word <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks word&#125;</sup> and had John beheaded in prison.

**11** His head was brought on a platter and given to the girl, and she took it to her mother.

**12** When John's <sup>&#123;Lit. his&#125;</sup> disciples came, they carried off the body and buried it. Then they went and told Jesus.

**13** <sup>&#123;Jesus Feeds More than Five Thousand People&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 6:30-44; Luke 9:10-17; John 6:1-14)&#125;</sup> d When Jesus heard this, he left that place and went <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks and went&#125;</sup> by boat to a deserted place by himself. The crowds heard of it and followed him on foot from the neighboring <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks neighboring&#125;</sup> towns.

**14** When he got out of the boat, <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks of the boat&#125;</sup> he saw a large crowd. He had compassion for them and healed their sick.

**15** When evening had come, the disciples went to him and said, "This is a deserted place, and it's already late. Send the crowds away so that they can go into the villages and buy food for themselves."

**16** But Jesus told them, "They don't need to go away. You give them something to eat."

**17** They told him, "We don't have anything here except five loaves of bread and two fish."

**18** He said, "Bring them to me."

**19** Then he ordered the crowds to sit down on the grass. Taking the five loaves and the two fish, he looked up to heaven and blessed them. Then he broke the loaves in pieces and gave them to his disciples, and the disciples gave them <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks gave them&#125;</sup> to the crowds.

**20** All of them ate and were filled. Then the disciples <sup>&#123;Lit. they&#125;</sup> picked up what was left of the broken pieces, twelve baskets full.

**21** Now those who had eaten were about 5, men, besides women and children.

**22** <sup>&#123;Jesus Walks on the Sea&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 6:45-52; John 6:16-21)&#125;</sup> d Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. He&#125;</sup> immediately had the disciples get into a boat and cross to the other side ahead of him, while he sent the crowds away.

**23** After dismissing the crowds, he went up on a hillside by himself to pray. When evening came, he was there alone.

**24** By this time the boat was in the middle of the sea <sup>&#123;Other mss. read many furlongs from the land&#125;</sup> and was being battered by the waves, because the wind was against them.

**25** Shortly before dawn <sup>&#123;Lit. In the fourth watch of the night&#125;</sup> Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. he&#125;</sup> came to them, walking on the sea.

**26** When the disciples saw him walking on the sea, they were terrified and cried out, "It's a ghost!" And they screamed in terror.

**27** "Have courage!" Jesus immediately told them. "It's me. Stop being afraid!"

**28** Peter answered him, "Lord, if it's you, order me to come to you on the water."

**29** Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. He&#125;</sup> said, "Come on!" So Peter got down out of the boat, started walking on the water, and came <sup>&#123;Other mss. read to go&#125;</sup> to Jesus.

**30** But when he noticed the strong <sup>&#123;Other mss. lack strong&#125;</sup> wind, he was frightened. As he began to sink, he shouted, "Lord, save me!"

**31** At once Jesus reached out his hand, caught him, and asked him, "You who have so little faith, why did you doubt?"

**32** As they got into the boat, the wind stopped blowing.

**33** Then the men in the boat began to worship Jesus, <sup>&#123;Lit. him&#125;</sup> saying, "You certainly are the Son of God!"

**34** <sup>&#123;Jesus Heals the Sick in Gennesaret&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 6:53-56)&#125;</sup> d They crossed over and came ashore at Gennesaret.

**35** When the men of that place recognized Jesus, <sup>&#123;Lit. him&#125;</sup> they sent word <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks word&#125;</sup> throughout that region and brought him everyone who was sick.

**36** They kept begging him to let them touch just the tassel of his garment, and everyone who touched it was completely healed.

## Chapter 15

**1** <sup>&#123;Jesus Challenges the Tradition of the Elders&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 7:1-23)&#125;</sup> d Then some Pharisees and scribes came from Jerusalem to Jesus and asked,

**2** "Why do your disciples disregard the tradition of the elders? They don't wash their hands when they eat." <sup>&#123;Lit. eat bread&#125;</sup>

**3** But he answered them, "Why do you also disregard the commandment of God because of your tradition?"

**4** Because God said, <sup>&#123;Other mss. read commanded, saying&#125;</sup> «Honor your father and your mother,' <sup>&#123;Exod 20:12; Deut 5:16&#125;</sup> and, «Whoever curses father or mother must certainly be put to death.'<sup>&#123;Exod 21:17; Lev 20:9&#125;</sup>

**5** But you say, «Whoever tells his father or his mother, "Whatever support you might have received from me has been given to God," <sup>&#123;Lit. is a gift&#125;</sup>

**6** does not have to honor his father.' <sup>&#123;Other mss. read his father or his mother&#125;</sup> Because of your tradition, then, you have disregarded <sup>&#123;Or invalidated&#125;</sup> the authority of God's word. <sup>&#123;Other mss. read law ; still other mss. read commandment&#125;</sup>

**7** You hypocrites! How well did Isaiah prophesy of you when he said,

**8** «These people honor me with their lips, but their hearts are far from me.

**9** Their worship of me is empty, because they teach human rules as doctrines.»"  <sup>&#123;Isa 29:13&#125;</sup>

**10** Then calling out to the crowd, he addressed them, "Listen and understand!

**11** It is not what goes into the mouth that makes a person unclean. It is what comes out of the mouth that makes a person unclean."

**12** Then the disciples came and asked him, "Do you realize that the Pharisees were offended when they heard this statement?"

**13** He replied, "Every plant that my heavenly Father did not plant will be pulled up by the roots.

**14** Leave them alone. They are blind guides of the blind. <sup>&#123;Other mss. lack of the blind&#125;</sup> If one blind person leads another blind person, both will fall into a ditch."

**15** Then Peter told him, "Explain to us this <sup>&#123;Other mss. read the&#125;</sup> parable."

**16** Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. He&#125;</sup> said, "Are you still so ignorant?

**17** Don't you know that everything that goes into the mouth passes into the stomach and then is expelled as waste?

**18** But the things that come out of the mouth come from the heart, and it is those things that make a person unclean.

**19** It is out of the heart that evil thoughts come, as well as murder, adultery, sexual immorality, stealing, false testimony, and slander.  <sup>&#123;Or blasphemy&#125;</sup>

**20** These are the things that make a person unclean. But eating with unwashed hands doesn't make a person unclean."

**21** <sup>&#123;A Canaanite Woman's Faith&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 7:24-30)&#125;</sup> d Then Jesus left that place and went to the region of Tyre and Sidon.

**22** Suddenly, a Canaanite woman from that territory came near and began to shout, "Have mercy on me, Lord, Son of David! My daughter is severely demon-possessed!"

**23** But he didn't answer her at all. <sup>&#123;Lit. a word&#125;</sup> Then his disciples came up and kept urging him, "Send her away, because she keeps on screaming as she follows <sup>&#123;Lit. screaming behind&#125;</sup> us."

**24** But he replied, "I was sent only to the lost sheep of the nation <sup>&#123;Lit. house&#125;</sup> of Israel."

**25** Then she came and fell down before him, saying, "Lord, help me!"

**26** He replied, "It's not right <sup>&#123;Other mss. read lawful&#125;</sup> to take the children's bread and throw it to the puppies."

**27** She said, "Yes, Lord. But even the puppies eat the crumbs that fall from their masters'tables."

**28** Then Jesus answered her, "Lady, <sup>&#123;Lit. Woman&#125;</sup> your faith is great! What you want is granted." That very hour her daughter was healed.

**29** <sup>&#123;Jesus Heals Many People&#125;</sup> d Jesus left there and went along the Sea of Galilee. Then he went up on a hillside and sat down.

**30** Large crowds came to him, bringing with them the lame, the blind, the crippled, those unable to talk, and many others. They placed them at his feet, and he healed them.

**31** As a result, the crowd was amazed to see those who were unable to talk speaking, the crippled healed, the lame walking, and the blind seeing. So they praised the God of Israel.

**32** <sup>&#123;Jesus Feeds More than Four Thousand People&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 8:1-10)&#125;</sup> d Then Jesus called his disciples and said, "I have compassion for the crowd because they have already been with me for three days and have nothing to eat. I don't want to send them away without food, or they may faint on the road."

**33** The disciples asked him, "Where in the wilderness are we to get enough bread to feed such a crowd?"

**34** Jesus asked them, "How many loaves of bread do you have?" They said, "Seven, and a few small fish."

**35** Ordering the crowd to sit down on the ground,

**36** he took the seven loaves and the fish and gave thanks. Then he broke them in pieces and kept giving them to his disciples, and the disciples gave them <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks gave them&#125;</sup> to the crowds.

**37** All of them ate until they were filled, then the disciples <sup>&#123;Lit. they&#125;</sup> picked up what was left of the broken pieces seven baskets full.

**38** Now those who had eaten were four thousand men, besides women and children.

**39** After he sent the crowds away, he got into a boat and went to the region of Magadan. <sup>&#123;Other mss. read Magdala&#125;</sup>

## Chapter 16

**1** <sup>&#123;Interpreting the Time&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 8:11-13; Luke 12:54-56)&#125;</sup> d When the Pharisees and Sadducees arrived, in order to test Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. him&#125;</sup> they asked him to show them a sign from heaven.

**2** He replied to them, "You say, «Red sky at night, what a delight!

**3** Red sky in the morning, cloudy and storming.» You know how to interpret the appearance of the sky, yet you can't interpret the signs of the times?  <sup>&#123;Other mss. lack You say. . . the signs of the times?&#125;</sup>

**4** An evil and adulterous generation craves a sign, but no sign will be given to it except the sign of Jonah." Then he left them and went away.

**5** <sup>&#123;The Yeast of the Pharisees and Sadducees&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 8:14-21)&#125;</sup> d When his disciples reached the other side, they had forgotten to take any bread along.

**6** Jesus told them, "Watch out! Beware of the yeast of the Pharisees and Sadducees!"

**7** As they began to discuss this among themselves, they kept saying, "We didn't bring along any bread."

**8** Knowing this, Jesus asked them, "You who have little faith, why are you discussing among yourselves the fact that you don't have any bread?

**9** Don't you understand yet? Don't you remember the five loaves for the 5, and how many baskets you collected,

**10** or the seven loaves for the 4, and how many baskets you collected?

**11** How can you fail to understand that I wasn't talking to you about bread? Beware of the yeast of the Pharisees and Sadducees!"

**12** Then they understood that he did not say to beware of the yeast used in bread, <sup>&#123;Other mss. read the loaf of bread&#125;</sup> but of the teaching of the Pharisees and Sadducees.

**13** <sup>&#123;Peter Declares His Faith in Jesus&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 8:27-30; Luke 9:18-21)&#125;</sup> d When Jesus had come to the region of Caesarea Philippi, he asked his disciples, "Who do people say the Son of Man is?"

**14** They said, "Some say <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks say&#125;</sup> John the Baptist, others Elijah, and still others Jeremiah or one of the prophets."

**15** He asked them, "But who do you say I am?"

**16** Simon Peter answered, "You are the Messiah, <sup>&#123;Or Christ&#125;</sup> the Son of the living God!"

**17** Then Jesus told him, "How blessed you are, Simon son of Jonah, <sup>&#123;Or Simon son of John; Lit. Simon bar Jonah; cf. John 1:42&#125;</sup> since flesh and blood has not revealed this to you, though my Father in heaven has.

**18** I tell you that you are Peter, <sup>&#123;Lit. Petros&#125;</sup> and it is on this rock <sup>&#123;Lit. petra&#125;</sup> that I will build my congregation, <sup>&#123;Or church&#125;</sup> and the powers of hell <sup>&#123;Lit. the gates of Hades, a reference to the realm of the dead&#125;</sup> will not conquer it.

**19** I will give you the keys to the kingdom from <sup>&#123;Lit. of&#125;</sup> heaven. Whatever you prohibit on earth will have been prohibited <sup>&#123;Or will be prohibited&#125;</sup> in heaven, and whatever you permit on earth will have been permitted <sup>&#123;Or will be permitted&#125;</sup> in heaven."

**20** Then he strictly ordered the disciples not to tell anyone that he was the Messiah. <sup>&#123;Or Christ&#125;</sup>

**21** <sup>&#123;Jesus Predicts His Death and Resurrection&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 8:31-9:1; Luke 9:21-27)&#125;</sup> d From that time on, Jesus began to show his disciples that he would have to go to Jerusalem and suffer a great deal because of the elders, the high priests, and the scribes. Then he would be killed, but on the third day he would be raised.

**22** Peter took him aside and began to rebuke him, saying, "God be merciful to you, Lord! This must never happen to you!"

**23** But Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. he&#125;</sup> turned and told Peter, "Get behind me, Satan! You are an offense <sup>&#123;Or a hindrance&#125;</sup> to me, because you are not thinking God's thoughts but human thoughts!"

**24** Then Jesus told his disciples, "If anyone wants to follow me, he must deny himself, pick up his cross, and follow me continually.

**25** Whoever wants to save his life will lose it, but whoever loses his life for my sake will find it,

**26** because what profit will a person have if he gains the whole world and forfeits his life? Or what can a person give in exchange for his life?

**27** The Son of Man is going to come with his angels in his Father's glory, and then he will repay everyone according to what he has done.

**28** I tell all of you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> with certainty, some people standing here will not experience <sup>&#123;Lit. taste&#125;</sup> death before they see the Son of Man coming in his kingdom."

## Chapter 17

**1** <sup>&#123;Jesus'Appearance is Changed&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 9:2-13; Luke 9:28-36)&#125;</sup> d Six days later, Jesus took Peter, James, and his brother John and led them up a high mountain by themselves.

**2** His appearance was changed in front of them, his face shone like the sun, and his clothes became as white as light.

**3** Suddenly, Moses and Elijah appeared to them, talking with Jesus. <sup>&#123;Lit. him&#125;</sup>

**4** Then Peter told Jesus, "Lord, it's good that we're here! If you want, I'll set up three shelters <sup>&#123;Or tents&#125;</sup> one for you, one for Moses, and one for Elijah."

**5** He was still speaking when a bright cloud suddenly overshadowed them. A voice from the cloud said, "This is my Son, whom I love. I am pleased with him. Keep on listening to him!"

**6** When the disciples heard this, they fell on their faces and were terrified.

**7** But Jesus came up to them and touched them, saying, "Get up, and stop being afraid."

**8** When they raised their eyes, they saw no one but Jesus all by himself.

**9** On their way down the mountain, Jesus ordered them, "Don't tell anyone about this vision until the Son of Man has been raised from the dead."

**10** So the disciples asked him, "Why, then, do the scribes say that Elijah must come first?"

**11** He answered them, "Elijah is indeed coming and will restore all things.

**12** But I tell you that Elijah has already come, yet people <sup>&#123;Lit. they&#125;</sup> did not recognize him and treated him just as they pleased. In the same way, the Son of Man is going to suffer at their hands."

**13** Then the disciples understood that he had been speaking to them about John the Baptist.

**14** <sup>&#123;Jesus Heals a Boy with a Demon&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 9:14-29; Luke 9:37-42)&#125;</sup> d As they approached the crowd, a man came up to Jesus, <sup>&#123;Lit. him&#125;</sup> knelt down in front of him,

**15** and said, "Sir, <sup>&#123;Or Lord&#125;</sup> have mercy on my son, because he is an epileptic and suffers terribly. Often he falls into fire and often into water.

**16** I brought him to your disciples, but they couldn't heal him."

**17** Jesus replied, "You unbelieving and perverted generation! How long must I be with you? How long must I put up with you? Bring him here to me!"

**18** Then Jesus rebuked the demon and it came out of him, and the boy was healed that very hour.

**19** Then the disciples came to Jesus privately and asked, "Why couldn't we drive it out?"

**20** He told them, "Because of your lack of faith. <sup>&#123;Other mss. read your little faith&#125;</sup> I tell all of you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> with certainty, if you have faith like a grain of mustard seed, you can say to this mountain, «Move from here to there,'and it will move, and nothing will be impossible for you.

**21** But this kind does not come out except by prayer and fasting." <sup>&#123;Other mss. lack this verse&#125;</sup>

**22** <sup>&#123;Jesus Again Predicts His Death and Resurrection&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 9:30-32; Luke 9:43-45)&#125;</sup> d While they were gathering together <sup>&#123;Other mss. read were staying&#125;</sup> in Galilee, Jesus told them, "The Son of Man is going to be betrayed into human hands.

**23** They will kill him, but he will be raised on the third day." Then they were filled with grief.

**24** <sup>&#123;Questions about the Temple Tax&#125;</sup> d When they came to Capernaum, the collectors of the temple tax <sup>&#123;Lit. didrachma&#125;</sup> came up to Peter and asked, "Your teacher pays the temple tax, <sup>&#123;Lit. didrachma&#125;</sup> doesn't he?"

**25** He answered, "Yes." When Peter <sup>&#123;Lit. he&#125;</sup> went home, <sup>&#123;Or went into the house&#125;</sup> Jesus spoke to him first and asked him, "What do you think, Simon? From whom do kings on the earth collect tolls or tributes? From their own subjects, <sup>&#123;Lit. sons&#125;</sup> or from foreigners?"

**26** "From foreigners," he replied. So Jesus told him, "In that case, the subjects <sup>&#123;Lit. sons&#125;</sup> are exempt.

**27** However, so that we don't offend them, go to the sea and throw in a hook. Take the first fish that comes up, open its mouth, and you will find a coin. <sup>&#123;Lit. stater, a coin worth two didrachmas&#125;</sup> Take it and give it to them for me and you."

## Chapter 18

**1** <sup>&#123;True Greatness&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 9:33-37; Luke 9:46-48)&#125;</sup> d At that time the disciples came to Jesus and asked, "Who, then, is the greatest in the kingdom from <sup>&#123;Lit. of&#125;</sup> heaven?"

**2** Calling a little child forward, he had him stand among them.

**3** Then he said, "I tell all of you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> with certainty, unless you change <sup>&#123;Lit. turn&#125;</sup> and become like little children, you will never get into the kingdom from <sup>&#123;Lit. of&#125;</sup> heaven.

**4** Therefore, whoever humbles himself as this little child is the greatest in the kingdom from <sup>&#123;Lit. of&#125;</sup> heaven,

**5** and whoever receives a little child like this in my name receives me."

**6** <sup>&#123;Causing Others to Sin&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 9:42-48; Luke 17:1-2)&#125;</sup> d "If anyone causes one of these little ones who believe in me to sin, it would be better for him if a large millstone were hung around his neck and he were drowned at the bottom of the sea.

**7** How terrible it will be for the world due to its temptations to sin! Temptations to sin are bound to happen, but how terrible it will be for that person who causes someone to sin!

**8** "So if your hand or your foot causes you to sin, cut it off and throw it away. It is better for you to enter life injured or crippled than to have two hands or two feet and be thrown into eternal fire.

**9** And if your eye causes you to sin, tear it out and throw it away. It is better for you to enter life with one eye than to have two eyes and be thrown into hell <sup>&#123;Lit. Gehenna ; a Gk. transliteration of the Heb. for Valley of Hinnom&#125;</sup> fire.

**10** "See to it that you do not despise one of these little ones, because I tell you, their angels in heaven always have access to my Father in heaven.

**11** For the Son of Man came to save the lost."  <sup>&#123;Other mss. lack this verse.&#125;</sup>

**12** <sup>&#123;The Parable about the Faithful Shepherd&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Luke 15:1-7)&#125;</sup> d "What do you think? If a man has 100 sheep and one of them strays, he leaves the 99 in the hills and goes to look for the one that has strayed, doesn't he?

**13** If he finds it, I tell all of you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> with certainty that he rejoices over it more than over the 99 that haven't strayed.

**14** In the same way, it is not the will of your <sup>&#123;Other mss. read our ; still other mss. read my&#125;</sup> Father in heaven that one of these little ones should be lost."

**15** <sup>&#123;Dealing with a Brother who Sins&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Luke 17:3)&#125;</sup> d "If your brother sins against you, <sup>&#123;Other mss. lack against you&#125;</sup> go and confront him while the two of you are alone. If he listens to you, you have won back your brother.

**16** But if he doesn't listen, take one or two others with you so that «every word may be confirmed by the testimony <sup>&#123;Lit. mouth&#125;</sup> of two or three witnesses.' <sup>&#123;Deut 19:15&#125;</sup>

**17** If, however, he ignores them, tell it to the congregation. <sup>&#123;Or church&#125;</sup> If he also ignores the congregation, <sup>&#123;Or church&#125;</sup> regard him as an unbeliever <sup>&#123;Lit. gentile ; i.e. an unbelieving non-Jew&#125;</sup> and a tax collector.

**18** "I tell all of you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> with certainty, whatever you prohibit on earth will have been prohibited <sup>&#123;Or will be prohibited&#125;</sup> in heaven, and whatever you permit on earth will have been permitted <sup>&#123;Or will be permitted&#125;</sup> in heaven.

**19** Furthermore, I tell all of you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> with certainty that if two of you agree on earth about anything you request, it will be done for you by my Father in heaven,

**20** because where two or three have come together in my name, I am there among them."

**21** <sup>&#123;The Parable about an Unforgiving Servant&#125;</sup> d Then Peter came up and asked him, "Lord, how many times may my brother sin against me and I have to forgive him? Seven times?"

**22** Jesus told him, "I tell you, not just seven times, but 77 times! <sup>&#123;Or seventy times seven&#125;</sup>

**23** "That is why the kingdom from <sup>&#123;Lit. of&#125;</sup> heaven may be compared to a king who wanted to settle accounts with his servants.

**24** When he had begun to settle the accounts, a person who owed him 10, talents <sup>&#123;A talent was worth a lifetime of wages for an average laborer.&#125;</sup> was brought to him.

**25** Because he couldn't pay, his master ordered him, his wife, his children, and everything that he owned to be sold so that payment could be made.

**26** Then the servant fell down and bowed low before him, saying, «Be patient <sup>&#123;Other mss. read Master, be patient&#125;</sup> with me, and I will repay you everything!»

**27** The master of that servant had compassion and released him, canceling his debt.

**28** "But when that servant went away, he found one of his fellow servants who owed him a hundred denarii. <sup>&#123;The denarius was the usual day's wage for a laborer.&#125;</sup> He grabbed him, seized him by the throat, and said, «Pay what you owe!'

**29** Then his fellow servant fell down and began begging him, «Be patient with me and I will repay you!»

**30** But he refused and went and had him thrown into prison until he could repay the debt.

**31** "When his fellow servants saw what had happened, they were very disturbed and went and reported to their master everything that had occurred.

**32** Then his master sent for him and told him, «You evil servant! I canceled that entire debt for you because you begged me.

**33** Shouldn't you have had mercy on your fellow servant, just as I had mercy on you?»

**34** In anger his master handed him over to the jailers until he could repay the entire debt.

**35** This is how my heavenly Father will treat each one of you unless you forgive your brother from your hearts."

## Chapter 19

**1** <sup>&#123;Teaching about Divorce&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 10:1-12)&#125;</sup> d When Jesus had finished saying these things, <sup>&#123;Lit. finished these sayings&#125;</sup> he left Galilee and went to the territory of Judea on the other side <sup>&#123;I.e. the east side&#125;</sup> of the Jordan.

**2** Large crowds followed him, and he healed them there.

**3** Some Pharisees came to him in order to test him. They asked, "Is it lawful for a man <sup>&#123;Other mss. lack for a man&#125;</sup> to divorce his wife for any reason?"

**4** He answered them, "Haven't you read that the one who made <sup>&#123;Other mss. read created&#125;</sup> them at the beginning «made them male and female'<sup>&#123;Gen 1:27; 5:2&#125;</sup>

**5** and said, «That is why a man will leave his father and mother and be united with his wife, and the two will become one flesh'?  <sup>&#123;Gen 2:24&#125;</sup>

**6** So they are no longer two, but one flesh. Therefore, what God has joined together, man must never separate."

**7** They asked him, "Why, then, did Moses order us «to give a certificate of divorce and divorce her'?" <sup>&#123;Deut 24:1, 3&#125;</sup>

**8** He told them, "It was because of your hardness of heart that Moses allowed you to divorce your wives. But from the beginning it was not this way.

**9** I tell you that whoever divorces his wife, except for sexual immorality, and marries another woman commits adultery."  <sup>&#123;Other mss. read adultery, and the man who marries a divorced woman commits adultery&#125;</sup>

**10** His disciples asked him, "If that is the relationship of a man with his wife, it's not worth getting married!"

**11** "Not everyone can accept this saying," he replied, "except those to whom celibacy <sup>&#123;Lit. it&#125;</sup> has been granted,

**12** because some men are celibate from birth, <sup>&#123;Lit. from the mother's womb&#125;</sup> while some are celibate because they have been made that way by others. Still others are celibate because they have made themselves that way for the sake of the kingdom from <sup>&#123;Lit. of&#125;</sup> heaven. Let anyone accept this who can."

**13** <sup>&#123;Jesus Blesses the Little Children&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 10:13-16; Luke 18:15-17)&#125;</sup> d Then some little children were brought to him so that he might lay his hands on them and pray. But the disciples rebuked those who brought <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks those who brought&#125;</sup> them.

**14** Jesus, however, said, "Let the little children come to me, and stop keeping them away, because the kingdom from <sup>&#123;Lit. of&#125;</sup> heaven belongs to people like these."

**15** When he had laid his hands on them, he went on from there.

**16** <sup>&#123;A Rich Man Comes to Jesus&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 10:17-22; Luke 18:18-23)&#125;</sup> d Just then a man came up to Jesus. <sup>&#123;Lit. him&#125;</sup> "Teacher,"<sup>&#123;Other mss. read Good Teacher&#125;</sup> he asked, "what good deed should I do to have eternal life?"

**17** Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. He&#125;</sup> asked him, "Why ask me about what is good? There is only one who is good. <sup>&#123;Other mss. read Why do you call me good? No one is good except for one God&#125;</sup> If you want to get into that life, you must keep the commandments."

**18** The young man <sup>&#123;Lit. He&#125;</sup> asked him, "Which ones?" Jesus said, «You must not murder, <sup>&#123;Exod 20:13; Deut 5:17&#125;</sup> you must not commit adultery, <sup>&#123;Exod 20:14; Deut 5:18&#125;</sup> you must not steal, <sup>&#123;Exod 20:15; Deut 5:19&#125;</sup> you must not give false testimony, <sup>&#123;Exod 20:16; Deut 5:20&#125;</sup>

**19** honor your father and mother,'<sup>&#123;Exod 20:12; Deut 5:16&#125;</sup> and, «you must love your neighbor as yourself.»" <sup>&#123;Lev 19:18&#125;</sup>

**20** The young man told him, "I have kept all of these. <sup>&#123;Other mss. read kept all of these since I was a young man&#125;</sup> What do I still lack?"

**21** Jesus told him, "If you want to be perfect, go and sell what you own and give the money <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks the money&#125;</sup> to the destitute, and you will have treasure in heaven. Then come back and follow me."

**22** But when the young man heard this statement he went away sad, because he had many possessions.

**23** <sup>&#123;Salvation and Reward&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 10:23-31; Luke 18:24-30)&#125;</sup> d Then Jesus told his disciples, "I tell all of you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> with certainty, it will be hard for a rich person to get into the kingdom from <sup>&#123;Lit. of&#125;</sup> heaven.

**24** Again I tell you, it is easier for a camel to squeeze through the eye of a needle than for a rich person to get into the kingdom of God."

**25** When the disciples heard this, they were completely astonished. "Who, then, can be saved?" they asked.

**26** Jesus looked at them intently and said, "For humans this is impossible, but for God all things are possible."

**27** "Look!" Peter replied. "We have left everything and followed you. So what will we get?"

**28** Jesus told them, "I tell all of you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> with certainty, when the Son of Man sits on his glorious throne in the renewed creation, you who have followed me will also sit on twelve thrones, governing the twelve tribes of Israel.

**29** In fact, everyone who has left his homes, brothers, sisters, father, mother, children, or fields because of my name will receive a hundred times as much <sup>&#123;Other mss. read many times as much&#125;</sup> and will inherit eternal life.

**30** But many who are first will be last, and the last will be first."

## Chapter 20

**1** <sup>&#123;The Workers in the Vineyard&#125;</sup> d "The kingdom from <sup>&#123;Lit. of&#125;</sup> heaven is like a landowner who went out early in the morning to hire workers for his vineyard.

**2** After agreeing to pay the workers one denarius <sup>&#123;The denarius was the usual day's wage for a laborer.&#125;</sup> a day, he sent them into his vineyard.

**3** When he went out about nine o'clock, <sup>&#123;Lit. the third hour&#125;</sup> he saw others standing in the marketplace without work.

**4** He told them, «You go into the vineyard, too, and I will pay you whatever is right.»

**5** So off they went. He went out again about noon <sup>&#123;Lit. the sixth hour&#125;</sup> and about three o'clock <sup>&#123;Lit. the ninth hour&#125;</sup> and did the same thing.

**6** About five o'clock <sup>&#123;Lit. the eleventh hour&#125;</sup> he went out and found some others standing around. He asked them, «Why are you standing here all day long without work?»

**7** They told him, «Because no one has hired us.'He told them, «You go into the vineyard as well.»

**8** "When evening came, the owner of the vineyard told his manager, «Call the workers and give them their wages, beginning with the last and ending with <sup>&#123;Lit. and up to&#125;</sup> the first.»

**9** Those who were hired at five o'clock <sup>&#123;Lit. the eleventh hour&#125;</sup> came, and each received a denarius.

**10** "When the first came, they thought they would receive more, but each received a denarius as well.

**11** When they received it, they began to complain to the landowner,

**12** «These last fellows worked only one hour, but you paid them the same as us, and we've been working all day, <sup>&#123;Lit. we've endured the burden of the day&#125;</sup> enduring the scorching heat!»

**13** "But he told one of them, «Friend, I'm not treating you unfairly. You did agree with me for a denarius, didn't you?

**14** Take what is yours and go. I want to give this last man as much as I gave you.  <sup>&#123;Lit. to this last man as also to you&#125;</sup>

**15** I am allowed to do what I want with my own money, <sup>&#123;Lit. things&#125;</sup> am I not? Or are you envious <sup>&#123;Lit. Or is your eye evil&#125;</sup> because I'm generous?»

**16** "In the same way, the last will be first, and the first will be last, because many are called, but few are chosen."  <sup>&#123;Other mss. lack For many are called, but few are chosen&#125;</sup>

**17** <sup>&#123;Jesus Predicts His Death and Resurrection a Third Time&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 10:32-34; Luke 18:31-34)&#125;</sup> d When Jesus was going up to Jerusalem, he took the twelve disciples <sup>&#123;Other mss. lack disciples&#125;</sup> aside and told them as they were walking along,

**18** "See, we are going up to Jerusalem, and the Son of Man will be handed over to the high priests and scribes, and they will condemn him to death.

**19** Then they will hand him over to unbelievers <sup>&#123;Lit. to the gentiles ; i.e. unbelieving non-Jews&#125;</sup> to be mocked, whipped, and crucified, but on the third day he will be raised."

**20** <sup>&#123;The Request of James and John&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 10:35-45)&#125;</sup> d Then the mother of Zebedee's sons came to Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. him&#125;</sup> with her sons. She bowed down in front of him to ask him for a favor.

**21** He asked her, "What do you want?" She told him, "Promise &#123;Lit. Say&#125; that in your kingdom these two sons of mine will sit on your right and on your left."

**22** Jesus replied, "You don't realize what you're asking. Can you drink from the cup that I'm going to drink from?" <sup>&#123;Other mss. read to drink from, or be baptized with the baptism with which I'm going to be baptized?&#125;</sup> They told him, "We can."

**23** He told them, "You will indeed drink from my cup. But it's not up to me to grant you a seat at my right hand or at my left. These positions have already been prepared for others by my Father."

**24** When the ten heard this, they became furious with the two brothers.

**25** But Jesus called the disciples <sup>&#123;Lit. them&#125;</sup> and said, "You know that the rulers of the unbelievers <sup>&#123;Lit. gentiles; i.e. unbelieving non-Jews&#125;</sup> lord it over them and their superiors act like tyrants over them.

**26** That's not the way it should be among you. Instead, whoever wants to be great among you must be your servant,

**27** and whoever wants to be first among you must be your slave.

**28** That's the way it is with the Son of Man. He did not come to be served, but to serve and to give his life as a ransom for many people."

**29** <sup>&#123;Jesus Heals Two Blind Men&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 10:46-52; Luke 18:35-43)&#125;</sup> d As they were leaving Jericho, a large crowd followed Jesus. <sup>&#123;Lit. him&#125;</sup>

**30** When two blind men who were sitting by the roadside heard that Jesus was passing by, they shouted, "Have mercy on us, Lord, <sup>&#123;Other mss. read Jesus&#125;</sup> Son of David!"

**31** When the crowd told them harshly to be silent, they shouted even louder, "Have mercy on us, Lord, Son of David!"

**32** Jesus stopped and called them, saying, "What do you want me to do for you?"

**33** They told him, "Lord, we want to be able to see!" <sup>&#123;Lit. Lord, that our eyes be opened&#125;</sup>

**34** Then Jesus, deeply moved with compassion, touched their eyes, and at once they could see again. So they followed him.

## Chapter 21

**1** <sup>&#123;The King Enters Jerusalem&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 11:1-11; Luke 19:28-38; John 12:12-19)&#125;</sup> d When they came near Jerusalem and had reached Bethphage on the Mount of Olives, Jesus sent two disciples on ahead and

**2** told them, "Go into the village ahead of you. At once you will find a donkey tied up and a colt with it. Untie them, and bring them to me.

**3** If anyone says anything to you, tell him, «The Lord needs them,'and that person will send them at once."

**4** Now this happened to fulfill what had been spoken through the prophet when he said,

**5** "Tell the daughter <sup>&#123;I.e. people&#125;</sup> of Zion, «Look, your king is coming to you! <sup>&#123;Isa 62:11&#125;</sup> He is humble and mounted on a donkey, even on a colt of a donkey.»" <sup>&#123;Zech 9:9&#125;</sup>

**6** So the disciples went and did as Jesus had directed them.

**7** They brought the donkey and the colt and put their coats on them, and he sat upon them.

**8** Many people in the crowd spread their own coats on the road, while others began cutting down branches from the trees and spreading them on the road.

**9** Both the crowds that went ahead of him and those that followed him kept shouting, "Hosanna <sup>&#123;Hosanna is Heb. for Please save or Praise.&#125;</sup> to the Son of David! How blessed is the one who comes in the name of the Lord! Hosanna in the highest heaven!" <sup>&#123;Ps 118:25-26; Ps 148:1&#125;</sup>

**10** When he came into Jerusalem, the whole city was trembling with excitement. The people <sup>&#123;Lit. They&#125;</sup> were asking, "Who is this?"

**11** The crowds kept saying, "This is the prophet Jesus, the man from Nazareth in Galilee."

**12** <sup>&#123;Confrontation in the Temple over Money&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 11:15-19; Luke 19:45-48; John 2:13-22)&#125;</sup> d Then Jesus went into the Temple, <sup>&#123;Other mss. read temple of God&#125;</sup> threw out everyone who was selling and buying in the Temple, and overturned the moneychangers'tables and the chairs of those who sold doves.

**13** He told them, "It is written, «My house is to be called a house of prayer,'<sup>&#123;Isa 56:7; Jer 7:11&#125;</sup> but you are turning it into a hideout <sup>&#123;Lit. cave&#125;</sup> for bandits!"

**14** Blind and lame people came to him in the Temple, and he healed them.

**15** But when the high priests and the scribes saw the amazing things that he had done and the children shouting in the Temple, "Hosanna <sup>&#123;Hosanna is Heb. for Please save or Praise.&#125;</sup> to the Son of David,"they became furious

**16** and asked him, "Do you hear what these people are saying?" Jesus told them, "Yes! Haven't you ever read, «From the mouths of infants and nursing babies you have created praise'?" <sup>&#123;Ps 8:2&#125;</sup>

**17** Then he left them and went out of the city to Bethany and spent the night there.

**18** <sup>&#123;Jesus Curses a Fig Tree&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 11:12-14, 20-24)&#125;</sup> d In the morning, as Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. he&#125;</sup> was returning to the city, he became hungry.

**19** Seeing a fig tree by the roadside, he went up to it but found nothing on it except leaves. He told it, "May fruit never come from you again!" And immediately the fig tree dried up.

**20** When the disciples saw this, they were amazed. "How did the fig tree dry up so quickly?" they asked.

**21** Jesus answered them, "I tell all of you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> with certainty, if you have faith and do not doubt, not only will you be able to do what has been done to the fig tree, but you will also say to this mountain, «Be removed and thrown into the sea,'and it will happen.

**22** You will receive whatever you ask for in prayer, if you believe."

**23** <sup>&#123;Jesus'Authority is Challenged&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 11:27-33; Luke 20:1-8)&#125;</sup> d Then Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. he&#125;</sup> went into the Temple. While he was teaching, the high priests and the elders of the people came to him and asked, "By what authority are you doing these things, and who gave you this authority?"

**24** Jesus answered them, "I, too, will ask you one question. <sup>&#123;Lit. one word&#125;</sup> If you answer it for me, I will also tell you by what authority I am doing these things.

**25** Where did John's authority to baptize <sup>&#123;Lit. John's baptism&#125;</sup> come from? From heaven or from humans?" They began discussing this among themselves, saying, "If we say, «From heaven,'he will say to us, «Then why didn't you believe him? '

**26** But if we say, «From humans,'we are afraid of the crowd, because everyone regards John as a prophet."

**27** So they told Jesus, "We don't know." He in turn told them, "Then I won't tell you by what authority I am doing these things."

**28** <sup>&#123;The Parable about Two Sons&#125;</sup> d "But what do you think? A man had two sons. He went to the first and said, «Son, go and work in the vineyard today.»

**29** His son <sup>&#123;Lit. He&#125;</sup> replied, «I don't want to,'but later he changed his mind and went.

**30** Then the father <sup>&#123;Lit. he&#125;</sup> went to the other son <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks son&#125;</sup> and told him the same thing. He replied, «I will, <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks will&#125;</sup> sir,'but he didn't go.

**31** Which of the two did the father's will?" They answered, "The first one." Jesus told them, "I tell all of you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> with certainty, tax collectors and prostitutes will get into God's kingdom ahead of you!

**32** John came to you living a righteous life, <sup>&#123;Lit. you in the way of righteousness&#125;</sup> and you didn't believe him, but the tax collectors and prostitutes did. But even when you saw that, you didn't change your minds <sup>&#123;Or didn't repent&#125;</sup> at last and believe him."

**33** <sup>&#123;The Parable about the Tenant Farmers&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 12:1-12; Luke 20:9-19)&#125;</sup> d "Listen to another parable. There was a landowner who planted a vineyard, put a wall around it, dug a wine press in it, and built a watchtower. Then he leased it to tenant farmers and went abroad.

**34** When harvest time approached, he sent his servants to the tenant farmers to collect his produce.

**35** But the farmers took his servants and beat one, killed another, and attacked another with stones.

**36** Again, he sent other servants to them, a greater number than the first, but the tenant farmers <sup>&#123;Lit. they&#125;</sup> treated them the same way.

**37** Finally, he sent his son to them, thinking, «They will respect my son.»

**38** But when the tenant farmers saw his son, they told one another, «This is the heir. Come on, let's kill him and get his inheritance!»

**39** So they grabbed him, threw him out of the vineyard, and killed him.

**40** Now when the owner of the vineyard returns, what will he do to those farmers?"

**41** They told him, "He will put those horrible men to a horrible death. Then he will lease the vineyard to other farmers who will give him his produce at harvest time."

**42** Jesus asked them, "Have you never read in the Scriptures, «The stone that the builders rejected has become the cornerstone. <sup>&#123;Or capstone&#125;</sup> This was the Lord's doing, and it is amazing in our eyes.'? <sup>&#123;Ps 118:22-23&#125;</sup>

**43** That is why I tell you that the kingdom of God will be taken away from you and given to a people who will produce fruit for it.

**44** The person who falls over this stone will be broken to pieces, but it will crush anyone on whom it falls."  <sup>&#123;Other mss. lack this verse&#125;</sup>

**45** When the high priests and the Pharisees heard his parables, they knew that he was talking about them.

**46** Although they wanted to arrest him, they were afraid of the crowds, who considered Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. him&#125;</sup> to be a prophet.

## Chapter 22

**1** <sup>&#123;The Parable about a Banquet&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Luke 14:15-24)&#125;</sup> d Again Jesus spoke to them in parables. He said,

**2** "The kingdom from <sup>&#123;Lit. of&#125;</sup> heaven may be compared to a king who gave a wedding banquet for his son.

**3** He sent his servants to call those who had been invited to the wedding, but they refused to come.

**4** So <sup>&#123;Lit. Again&#125;</sup> he sent other servants, saying, «Tell those who have been invited, "Look, I've prepared my dinner. My oxen and fattened calves have been slaughtered. Everything is ready. Come to the wedding!"»

**5** But they paid no attention to this and went away, one to his farm, another to his business.

**6** The rest grabbed the king's <sup>&#123;Lit. his&#125;</sup> servants, treated them brutally, and then killed them.

**7** Then the king became outraged. He sent his troops, and they destroyed those murderers and burned their city.

**8** "Then he told his servants, «The wedding is ready, but those who were invited were not worthy.

**9** So go into the roads leading out of town and invite as many people as you can find to the wedding.»

**10** Those servants went out into the streets and brought in everyone they found, evil and good alike, and the wedding hall was packed with guests.

**11** "When the king came in to see the guests, he noticed a man there who was not wearing wedding clothes.

**12** He asked him, «Friend, how did you get in here without wedding clothes? 'But the man <sup>&#123;Lit. he&#125;</sup> was speechless.

**13** Then the king told his servants, «Tie his hands and feet, and throw him into the darkness outside!'In that place there will be weeping and gnashing of teeth,  <sup>&#123;I.e. extreme pain&#125;</sup>

**14** because many are invited, but few are chosen."

**15** <sup>&#123;A Question about Paying Taxes&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 12:13-17; Luke 20:20-26)&#125;</sup> d Then the Pharisees went and planned how to trap Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. him&#125;</sup> in conversation.

**16** They sent their disciples to him along with the Herodians. <sup>&#123;I.e. Royal party sympathizers&#125;</sup> They said, "Teacher, we know that you are sincere and that you teach the way of God truthfully. You don't favor any individual, because you pay no attention to external appearance.

**17** So tell us what you think. Is it lawful to pay taxes to Caesar or not?"

**18** Recognizing their wickedness, Jesus asked, "Why are you testing me, you hypocrites?

**19** Show me the coin used for the tax." They brought him a denarius. <sup>&#123;The denarius was the usual day's wage for a laborer.&#125;</sup>

**20** Then he asked them, "Whose face and name is this?"

**21** They told him, "Caesar's." So he told them, "Then give back to Caesar the things that are Caesar's, and to God the things that are God's."

**22** When they heard this, they were amazed. Then they left him and went away.
