**23** <sup>&#123;A Question about the Resurrection&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 12:18-27; Luke 20:27-40)&#125;</sup> d That same day some Sadducees, who claim there is no resurrection, came to Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. him&#125;</sup> and asked him,

**24** "Teacher, Moses said, «If a man dies having no children, his brother must marry the widow and have children for his brother.'<sup>&#123;Deut 25:5-6&#125;</sup>

**25** Now there were seven brothers among us. The first one married and died, and since he had no children, he left his widow to his brother.

**26** The same thing happened with the second brother, and then the third, and finally with the rest of the brothers. <sup>&#123;Lit. with the seven&#125;</sup>

**27** Finally, the woman died, too.

**28** Now in the resurrection, whose wife of the seven will she be, since all of them had married <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks married&#125;</sup> her?"

**29** Jesus answered them, "You are mistaken because you don't know the Scriptures or God's power,

**30** because in the resurrection, people <sup>&#123;Lit. they&#125;</sup> neither marry nor are given in marriage but are like the angels <sup>&#123;Other mss. read God's angels&#125;</sup> in heaven.

**31** As for the resurrection from the dead, haven't you read what was spoken to you by God when he said,

**32** «I am the God of Abraham, the God of Isaac, and the God of Jacob'? <sup>&#123;Exod 3:6&#125;</sup> He <sup>&#123;Other mss. read God&#125;</sup> is not the God of the dead, but of the living."

**33** When the crowds heard this, they were amazed at his teaching.

**34** <sup>&#123;The Greatest Commandment&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 12:28-34; Luke 10:25-28)&#125;</sup> d When the Pharisees heard that Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. he&#125;</sup> had silenced the Sadducees, they met together in the same place.

**35** One of them, an expert in the Law, tested him by asking,

**36** "Teacher, which is the greatest commandment in the Law?"

**37** Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. He&#125;</sup> told him, «You must love the Lord your God with all your heart, with all your soul, and with all your mind.'<sup>&#123;Deut 6:5&#125;</sup>

**38** This is the greatest and most important <sup>&#123;Or first&#125;</sup> commandment.

**39** The second is exactly like it: «You must love your neighbor as yourself.' <sup>&#123;Lev 19:18&#125;</sup>

**40** All the Law and the Prophets depend on these two commandments."

**41** <sup>&#123;A Question about David's Son&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 12:35-37; Luke 20:41-44)&#125;</sup> d While the Pharisees were still <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks still&#125;</sup> gathered, Jesus asked them,

**42** "What do you think about the Messiah? <sup>&#123;Or Christ&#125;</sup> Whose son is he?" They told him, "David's."

**43** He asked them, "Then how can David by the Spirit call him «Lord'when he says,

**44** «The Lord told my Lord, "Sit at my right hand, until I put your enemies under your feet."'?  <sup>&#123;Ps 110:1&#125;</sup>

**45** If David calls him «Lord', how can he be his son?"

**46** No one could answer him at all, <sup>&#123;Lit. a word&#125;</sup> and from that day on no one dared to ask him another question.

## Chapter 23

**1** <sup>&#123;Jesus Denounces the Scribes and the Pharisees&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 12:38-40; Luke 20:45-47)&#125;</sup> d Then Jesus told the crowds and his disciples,

**2** "The scribes and the Pharisees administer the authority of Moses,  <sup>&#123;Lit. Pharisees sit in Moses'seat&#125;</sup>

**3** So do whatever they tell you and follow it, but stop doing what they do, because they don't do what they say.

**4** They tie up burdens that are heavy and unbearable and lay them on people's shoulders, but they refuse to lift a finger to remove them.

**5** "They do everything to be seen by people. They increase the size of their phylacteries <sup>&#123;I.e. leather cases containing Scripture texts&#125;</sup> and lengthen the tassels of their garments.

**6** They love to have the places of honor at festivals, the best seats in the synagogues,

**7** to be greeted in the marketplaces, and to be called «Rabbi'<sup>&#123;Rabbi is Heb. for Master and/or Teacher .&#125;</sup> by people.

**8** "But you are not to be called «Rabbi,'<sup>&#123;Rabbi is Heb. for Master and/or Teacher&#125;</sup> because you have only one teacher, and all of you are brothers.

**9** And don't call anyone on earth «Father,'because you have only one Father, the one in heaven.

**10** Nor are you to be called «Teachers,'because you have only one teacher, the Messiah!  <sup>&#123;Or Christ&#125;</sup>

**11** The person who is greatest among you must be your servant.

**12** Whoever exalts himself will be humbled, and whoever humbles himself will be exalted.

**13** "How terrible it will be for you, scribes and Pharisees, you hypocrites! You shut the door to the kingdom from <sup>&#123;Lit. of&#125;</sup> heaven in people's faces. You don't go in yourselves, and you don't allow those who are trying to enter to go in.

**14** "How terrible it will be for you, scribes and Pharisees, you hypocrites! You devour widows'houses and say long prayers to cover it up. Therefore, you will receive greater condemnation!  <sup>&#123;Other mss. omit vs. 14.&#125;</sup>

**15** "How terrible it will be for you, scribes and Pharisees, you hypocrites! You travel over land and sea to make a single convert, and when this happens you make him twice as fit for <sup>&#123;Lit. twice as much a son of&#125;</sup> hell <sup>&#123;Lit. Gehenna ; a Gk. transliteration of the Heb. for Valley of Hinnom&#125;</sup> as you are.

**16** "How terrible it will be for you, blind guides! You say, «Whoever swears an oath by the sanctuary is excused, <sup>&#123;Lit. is nothing&#125;</sup> but whoever swears an oath by the gold of the sanctuary must keep his oath.' <sup>&#123;Lit. owes a debt&#125;</sup>

**17** You blind fools! What is more important, the gold or the sanctuary that made the gold holy?

**18** Again you say, <sup>&#123;Lit. And&#125;</sup> «Whoever swears an oath by the altar is excused, <sup>&#123;Lit. is nothing&#125;</sup> but whoever swears by the gift that is on it must keep his oath.' <sup>&#123;Lit. owes a debt&#125;</sup>

**19** You blind men! <sup>&#123;Other mss. read blind and foolish men&#125;</sup> Which is more important, the gift or the altar that makes the gift holy?

**20** Therefore, the one who swears an oath by the altar swears by it and by everything on it.

**21** The one who swears an oath by the sanctuary swears by it and by the one who lives there.

**22** And the one who swears an oath by heaven swears by God's throne and by the one who sits on it.

**23** "How terrible it will be for you, scribes and Pharisees, you hypocrites! You give a tenth of your mint, dill, and cummin, but have neglected the more important matters of the Law: justice, mercy, and faithfulness. <sup>&#123;Or faith&#125;</sup> These are the things you should have practiced, without neglecting the others.

**24** You blind guides! You filter out a gnat, yet swallow a camel!

**25** "How terrible it will be for you, scribes and Pharisees, you hypocrites! You clean the outside of the cup and the plate, but on the inside they are full of greed and self-indulgence.

**26** You blind Pharisee! First clean the inside of the cup, <sup>&#123;Other mss. read of the cup and the plate&#125;</sup> so that its outside may also be clean.

**27** "How terrible it will be for you, scribes and Pharisees, you hypocrites! You are like whitewashed tombs that look beautiful on the outside but inside are full of dead people's bones and every kind of impurity.

**28** In the same way, on the outside you look righteous to people, but inside you are full of hypocrisy and lawlessness.

**29** "How terrible it will be for you, scribes and Pharisees, you hypocrites! You build tombs for the prophets and decorate the monuments of the righteous.

**30** Then you say, «If we had been living in the days of our ancestors, we would have had no part with them in shedding <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks shedding&#125;</sup> the blood of the prophets.»

**31** Therefore, you testify against yourselves that you are descendants of those who murdered the prophets.

**32** Then finish what your ancestors started!  <sup>&#123;Lit. Fill up the measure of your ancestors&#125;</sup>

**33** You snakes, you children of serpents! How can you escape being condemned to hell?  <sup>&#123;Lit. Gehenna ; a Gk. transliteration of the Heb. for Valley of Hinnom&#125;</sup>

**34** "That is why I am sending you prophets, wise men, and scribes. Some of them you will kill and crucify, and some of them you will whip in your synagogues and persecute from town to town.

**35** As a result, you will be held accountable for <sup>&#123;Lit. on you will come&#125;</sup> all the righteous blood shed on earth, from the blood of the righteous Abel <sup>&#123;Gen 4:8&#125;</sup> to the blood of Zechariah, <sup>&#123;2Chr 24:20-21&#125;</sup> the son of Berechiah, whom you murdered between the sanctuary and the altar.

**36** I tell all of you  <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> with certainty, all these things will happen to those living today." <sup>&#123;Lit. to this generation&#125;</sup>

**37** <sup>&#123;Jesus Rebukes Jerusalem&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Luke 13:34-35)&#125;</sup> d "O Jerusalem, Jerusalem, who kills the prophets and stones to death those who have been sent to her! How often I wanted to gather your children together as a hen gathers her chicks under her wings, but you were unwilling!

**38** Look! Your house is left abandoned!

**39** I tell you, you will not see me again until you say, «How blessed is the one who comes in the name of the Lord!»"  <sup>&#123;Ps 118:26&#125;</sup>

## Chapter 24

**1** <sup>&#123;Jesus Predicts the Destruction of the Temple&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 13:1-2; Luke 21:5-6)&#125;</sup> d As Jesus left the Temple and was walking away, his disciples came up to him to point out to him the Temple buildings.

**2** But he told them, "You see all these things, don't you? I tell all of you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> with certainty, there isn't a single stone here that will be left standing on top of another. They will all be torn down."

**3** <sup>&#123;Cults, Revolutions, Famines, and Earthquakes&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 13:3-13; Luke 21:7-19)&#125;</sup> d While Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. he&#125;</sup> was sitting on the Mount of Olives, the disciples came to him privately and said, "Tell us, when will these things take place, and what will be the sign of your coming and of the end of the age?"

**4** Jesus answered them, "See to it that no one deceives you,

**5** because many will come in my name and say, «I am the Messiah,'<sup>&#123;Or Christ&#125;</sup> and they will deceive many people.

**6** You are going to hear of wars and rumors of wars. See to it that you are not alarmed. These things must take place, but the end hasn't come yet,

**7** because nation will rise up in arms against nation, and kingdom against kingdom. There will be famines and earthquakes in various places.

**8** But all these things are only the beginning of the birth pains."

**9** <sup>&#123;Future Persecution, Apostasy, and Evangelism&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 13:9-13; Luke 21:12-19)&#125;</sup> d "Then they will hand you over to suffer <sup>&#123;Or tribulation&#125;</sup> and will kill you, and you will be hated by all the nations <sup>&#123;Or gentiles&#125;</sup> because of my name.

**10** Then many people will fall away, will betray one another, and will hate one another.

**11** Many false prophets will appear and deceive many people,

**12** and because lawlessness will increase, the love of many people will grow cold.

**13** But the person who endures to the end will be saved.

**14** And this gospel of the kingdom will be proclaimed throughout the world as a testimony to all nations, <sup>&#123;Or gentiles&#125;</sup> and then the end will come."

**15** <sup>&#123;Signs of the End&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 13:14-23; Luke 21:20-24)&#125;</sup> d "So when you see the destructive desecration, mentioned by the prophet Daniel, standing in the Holy Place (let the reader take note) , <sup>&#123;Dan 9:27; 11:31; 12:11&#125;</sup>

**16** then those who are in Judea must flee to the mountains.

**17** Anyone who's on the housetop must not come down to get what is in his house,

**18** and anyone who's in the field must not turn back to get his coat.

**19** "How terrible it will be for women who are pregnant or who are nursing babies in those days!

**20** Pray that it may not be in winter or on a Sabbath when you flee,

**21** because at that time there will be great suffering, <sup>&#123;Or tribulation&#125;</sup> the kind that has not happened from the beginning of the world until now and certainly will never happen again.

**22** If those days had not been limited, no one <sup>&#123;Lit. flesh&#125;</sup> would survive. But for the sake of the elect, those days will be limited.

**23** "At that time, if anyone says to you, «Look! Here is the Messiah!'<sup>&#123;Or Christ&#125;</sup> or «There he is!', don't believe it,

**24** because false messiahs <sup>&#123;Or christs&#125;</sup> and false prophets will appear and display great signs and wonders to deceive, if possible, even the elect.

**25** Remember, I have told you this beforehand.

**26** So if they say to you, «Look! He's in the wilderness,'don't go out looking for him. <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks looking for him&#125;</sup> And if they say, «Look! He's in the storeroom,'don't believe it,

**27** because just as the lightning comes from the east and flashes as far as the west, so will be the coming of the Son of Man.

**28** Wherever there's a corpse, there the vultures <sup>&#123;Or eagles&#125;</sup> will gather.

**29** <sup>&#123;The Coming of the Son of Man&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 13:24-27; Luke 21:25-28)&#125;</sup> d "Immediately after the troubles <sup>&#123;Or tribulation&#125;</sup> of those days, «The sun will be darkened, the moon will not give its light, the stars will fall from the sky, and the powers of heaven will be shaken loose.' <sup>&#123;Isa 13:10; 34:4; Joel 2:10&#125;</sup>

**30** Then the sign of the Son of Man will appear in the sky, and all «the tribes of the land <sup>&#123;Or earth&#125;</sup> will mourn'<sup>&#123;Zech 12:12&#125;</sup> when they see «the Son of Man coming on the clouds of heaven'<sup>&#123;Dan 7:13&#125;</sup> with power and great glory.

**31** He will send out his angels with a loud trumpet blast, and they will gather his elect from the four winds, from one end of heaven to another."

**32** <sup>&#123;The Lesson from the Fig Tree&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 13:28-31; Luke 21:29-33)&#125;</sup> d "Now learn a lesson <sup>&#123;Or parable&#125;</sup> from the fig tree. When its branches become tender and it produces leaves, you know that summer is near.

**33** In the same way, when you see all these things, you will know that the Son of Man <sup>&#123;Lit. that he&#125;</sup> is near, right at the door.

**34** I tell all of you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> with certainty, this generation will not disappear until these things happen.

**35** Heaven and earth will disappear, but my words will never disappear."

**36** <sup>&#123;The Unknown Day and Hour of Messiah's Return&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 13:32-37; Luke 17:20-36)&#125;</sup> d "No one knows when that day or hour will come <sup>&#123;Lit. about that day and hour&#125;</sup> not the angels in heaven, nor the Son, <sup>&#123;Other mss. lack nor the Son&#125;</sup> but only the Father,

**37** because just as it was in the days of Noah, so it will be when the Son of Man comes.

**38** In those days before the flood, people <sup>&#123;Lit. they&#125;</sup> were eating and drinking, marrying and giving in marriage right up to the day when Noah went into the ark.

**39** They were unaware of what was happening <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks of what was happening&#125;</sup> until the flood came and swept all of them away. That's how it will be when the Son of Man comes.

**40** At that time two people will be in the field. One will be taken, and one will be left behind.

**41** Two women will be grinding grain <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks grain&#125;</sup> at the mill. One will be taken, and one will be left behind.

**42** "So keep on watching, because you don't know on what day your Lord is coming.

**43** But be sure of this: if the owner of the house had known when during <sup>&#123;Lit. known at what watch of&#125;</sup> the night the thief would be coming, he would have stayed awake and not allowed his house to be broken into.

**44** So you, too, must be ready, because at an hour you are not expecting him the Son of Man will come."

**45** <sup>&#123;The Faithful or the Wicked Servant&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Luke 12:42-48)&#125;</sup> d "Who, then, is the faithful and wise servant whom his master has put in charge of his household to give the others <sup>&#123;Lit. them&#125;</sup> their food at the right time?

**46** How blessed is that servant whom his master finds doing this when he comes!

**47** I tell all of you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> with certainty, he will put him in charge of all his property.

**48** "But if that wicked servant says to himself, <sup>&#123;Lit. in his heart&#125;</sup> «My master has been delayed,»

**49** and begins to beat his fellow servants and eat and drink with the drunks,

**50** the master of that servant will come on a day when he doesn't expect him and at an hour that he doesn't know.

**51** Then his master  <sup>&#123;Lit. he&#125;</sup> will punish him severely <sup>&#123;Lit. will cut him in pieces&#125;</sup> and assign him a place with the hypocrites. In that place there will be weeping and gnashing of teeth." <sup>&#123;I.e. extreme pain and anger&#125;</sup>

## Chapter 25

**1** <sup>&#123;The Parable about the Ten Bridesmaids&#125;</sup> d "At that time, the kingdom from <sup>&#123;Lit. of&#125;</sup> heaven will be comparable to ten bridesmaids <sup>&#123;Lit. virgins&#125;</sup> who took their oil lamps and went out to meet the groom. <sup>&#123;Other mss. read the groom and the bride&#125;</sup>

**2** Now five of them were foolish, and five were wise,

**3** because when the foolish ones took their lamps, they didn't take any oil with them.

**4** But the wise ones took flasks of oil with their lamps.

**5** Since the groom was late, all of them became sleepy and lay down.

**6** "But at midnight there came a shout: «The groom is here! Come out to meet him!»

**7** Then all the bridesmaids <sup>&#123;Lit. virgins&#125;</sup> woke up and got their lamps ready.

**8** But the foolish ones told the wise, «Give us some of your oil, because our lamps are going out!»

**9** But the wise ones replied, «No! There will never be enough for us and for you. You'd better go to the dealers and buy some for yourselves.»

**10** "While they were away buying it, the groom arrived. Those who were ready went with him into the wedding banquet, and the door was closed.

**11** Later, the other bridesmaids <sup>&#123;Lit. virgins&#125;</sup> arrived and said, «Lord, lord, open up for us!»

**12** But he replied, «I tell all of you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> with certainty, I don't know you!»

**13** So keep on watching, because you don't know the day or the hour."  <sup>&#123;Other mss. read the hour when the Son of Man will come&#125;</sup>

**14** <sup>&#123;The Parable about the Talents&#125;</sup> d "Similarly, it is like a man going on a trip who called his servants and turned his money over to them.

**15** To one man he gave five talents, <sup>&#123;A talent was worth a lifetime of wages for an average laborer.&#125;</sup> to another two, and to another one, based on their ability. Then he went on his trip.

**16** "The one who received five talents went out at once and invested them and earned five more.

**17** In the same way, the one who had two talents earned two more.

**18** But the one who received one talent went off, dug a hole in the ground, and buried his master's money.

**19** "After a long time, the master of those servants returned and settled accounts with them.

**20** The one who had received five talents came up and brought five more talents, saying, «Master, you gave me five talents. See, I've earned five more talents.»

**21** His master told him, «Well done, good and trustworthy servant! Since you have been trustworthy with a small amount, I will put you in charge of a large amount. Come and share your master's joy!»

**22** "The one with two talents also came forward and said, «Master, you gave me two talents. See, I've earned two more talents.»

**23** His master told him, «Well done, good and trustworthy servant! Since you have been trustworthy with a small amount, I will put you in charge of a large amount. Come and share your master's joy!»

**24** "Then the one who had received one talent came forward and said, «Master, I knew that you were a hard man, harvesting where you haven't planted and gathering where you haven't scattered any seed.

**25** Being afraid, I went off and hid your talent in the ground. Here, take what is yours!»

**26** "His master answered him, «You evil and lazy servant! So you knew that I harvested where I haven't planted and gathered where I haven't scattered any seed?

**27** Then you should have invested my money with the bankers. When I returned, I would have received my money back with interest.»

**28** Then the master said, <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks «Then the master said'&#125;</sup> «Take the talent from him and give it to the man who has the ten talents,

**29** because to everyone who has something, more will be given, and he will have more than enough. But from the person who has nothing, even what he has will be taken away from him.

**30** Throw this useless servant into the darkness outside! In that place there will be weeping and gnashing of teeth.»"  <sup>&#123;I.e. extreme pain&#125;</sup>

**31** <sup>&#123;The Judgment of the Nations&#125;</sup> d "When the Son of Man comes in his glory and all the angels are with him, he will sit on his glorious throne.

**32** All the nations will be assembled in front of him, and he will cull them out, one from another, like a shepherd separates sheep from goats.

**33** He will put the sheep on his right but the goats on his left.

**34** "Then the king will say to those on his right, «Come, you who have been blessed by my Father! Inherit the kingdom prepared for you from the foundation of the world,

**35** because I was hungry, and you gave me something to eat. I was thirsty, and you gave me something to drink. I was a stranger, and you welcomed me.

**36** I was naked, and you clothed me. I was sick, and you took care of me. I was in prison, and you visited me.»

**37** "Then the righteous will say to him, «Lord, when did we see you hungry and give you something to eat, or thirsty and give you something to drink?

**38** When did we see you as a stranger and welcome you, or see you naked and clothe you?

**39** When did we see you sick or in prison and visit you?»

**40** The king will answer them, «I tell all of you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> with certainty, since you did it for one of the least important of these brothers of mine, you did it for me.»

**41** "Then he will say to those on his left, «Get away from me, you who are accursed, into the eternal fire that has been prepared for the devil and his angels!

**42** Here's why: I was hungry, and you gave me nothing to eat. I was thirsty, and you gave me nothing to drink.

**43** I was a stranger, and you didn't welcome me. I was naked, and you didn't clothe me. I was sick and in prison, and you didn't visit me.»

**44** "Then they will reply, «Lord, when did we see you hungry or thirsty or as a stranger or naked or sick or in prison and didn't help you?»

**45** Then he will say to them, «I tell all of you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> with certainty, since you didn't do it for one of the least important of these, you didn't do it for me.»

**46** These people will go away into eternal punishment, but the righteous will go <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks will go&#125;</sup> into eternal life."

## Chapter 26

**1** <sup>&#123;The Plot to Kill Jesus&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 14:1-2; Luke 22:1-2; John 11:45-53)&#125;</sup> d When Jesus had finished saying all these things, <sup>&#123;Lit. finished all these sayings&#125;</sup> he told his disciples,

**2** "You know that the Passover will take place in two days, and the Son of Man will be handed over to be crucified."

**3** Then the high priests and the elders of the people assembled in the courtyard of the high priest, who was named Caiaphas.

**4** They conspired to arrest Jesus by treachery and to kill him.

**5** But they kept saying, "This must not happen during the festival. Otherwise, there'll be a riot among the people."

**6** <sup>&#123;A Woman Anoints Jesus&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 14:3-9; John 12:1-8)&#125;</sup> d While Jesus was in Bethany at the home of Simon the leper,

**7** a woman came to him with an alabaster jar of very expensive perfume and poured it on his head while he sat at the table.

**8** But when the disciples saw this they became irritated and said, "Why this waste?

**9** Surely this perfume could have been sold for a high price and the money <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks the money&#125;</sup> given to the destitute."

**10** But knowing this, <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks this&#125;</sup> Jesus asked them, "Why are you bothering the woman? She has done a beautiful thing for me.

**11** You will always have the destitute with you, but you will not always have me.

**12** When she poured this perfume on my body, she was preparing me for burial.

**13** I tell all of you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> with certainty, wherever this gospel is proclaimed throughout the whole world, what she has done will also be told as a memorial to her."

**14** <sup>&#123;Judas Agrees to Betray Jesus&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 14:10-11; Luke 22:3-6)&#125;</sup> d Then one of the Twelve, who was called Judas Iscariot, went to the high priests

**15** and inquired, "What are you willing to give me if I betray Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. him&#125;</sup> to you?" They offered him 30 pieces of silver,

**16** and from then on he began to look for an opportunity to betray Jesus. <sup>&#123;Lit. him&#125;</sup>

**17** <sup>&#123;The Passover with the Disciples&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 14:12-21; Luke 22:7-14, 21-23; John 12:21-30)&#125;</sup> d On the first day of the Festival <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks day of the Festival&#125;</sup> of Unleavened Bread, the disciples approached Jesus and asked, "Where do you want us to make preparations for you to eat the Passover meal?"

**18** He said, "Go to a certain man in the city and say to him, «The Teacher says, "My time is near. I will celebrate the Passover with my disciples at your house."»" <sup>&#123;Lit. with you&#125;</sup>

**19** So the disciples did as Jesus had directed them, and they prepared the Passover meal.

**20** When evening came, Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. he&#125;</sup> was sitting at the table with the Twelve. <sup>&#123;Other mss. read twelve disciples&#125;</sup>

**21** While they were eating, he said, "I tell all of you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> with certainty, one of you is going to betray me."

**22** Feeling deeply distressed, each one began to ask him, "Surely I am not the one, Lord?"

**23** He replied, "The man who has dipped his hand into the bowl with me will betray me.

**24** The Son of Man is going away, just as it has been written about him. How terrible it will be for that man by whom the Son of Man is betrayed! It would have been better for him if he had never been born."

**25** Then Judas, who was going to betray him, asked, "Rabbi, &#123;Rabbi is Heb. for Master and/or Teacher .&#125; I'm not the one, am I?" Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. He&#125;</sup> told him, "You have said so."

**26** <sup>&#123;The Lord's Supper&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 14:22-26; Luke 22:15-20)&#125;</sup> d While they were eating, Jesus took a loaf of bread and blessed it. Then he broke it in pieces and handed it to the disciples, saying, "Take this and eat it. This is my body."

**27** Then he took a cup, gave thanks, and gave it to them, saying, "Drink from it, all of you,

**28** because this is my blood of the new <sup>&#123;Other mss. lack new&#125;</sup> covenant that is being poured out for many people for the forgiveness of sins.

**29** I tell all of you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is pl.&#125;</sup> I will never again drink the product of the vine until that day when I drink it with you once again in my Father's kingdom."

**30** After singing a hymn, they went out to the Mount of Olives.

**31** <sup>&#123;Jesus Predicts Peter's Denial&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 14:27-31; Luke 22:31-34; John 13:36-38)&#125;</sup> d Then Jesus told them, "All of you will turn against me this very night, because it is written, «I will strike the shepherd, and the sheep of the flock will be scattered.' <sup>&#123;Zech 13:7&#125;</sup>

**32** However, after I have been raised, I will go to Galilee ahead of you."

**33** But Peter told him, "Even if everyone else turns against you, I certainly won't!"

**34** Jesus told him, "I tell you <sup>&#123;The Gk. pronoun you is sing.&#125;</sup> with certainty, before a rooster crows this very night, you will deny me three times."

**35** Peter told him, "Even if I have to die with you, I will never deny you!" And all the disciples said the same thing.

**36** <sup>&#123;Jesus Prays in the Garden of Gethsemane&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 14:32-42; Luke 22:39-46)&#125;</sup> d Then Jesus went with them to a place called Gethsemane. He told the disciples, "Sit down here while I go over there and pray."

**37** Taking Peter and the two sons of Zebedee with him, he began to be grieved and troubled.

**38** Then he told them, "I'm so deeply grieved that I feel I'm about to die. Wait here and stay awake with me."

**39** Going on a little farther, he fell on his face and prayed, "O my Father, if it is possible, let this cup pass from me. Yet not what I want but what you want."

**40** When he went back to the disciples, he found them asleep. He told Peter, "So, you men couldn't stay awake with me for one hour, could you?

**41** All of you must stay awake and pray that you won't be tempted. The spirit is indeed willing, but the body <sup>&#123;Lit. flesh&#125;</sup> is weak."

**42** He went away a second time and prayed, "My Father, if this cup cannot go away unless I drink it, let your will be done."

**43** Then he came back and found them asleep, because they could not keep their eyes open.

**44** After leaving them again, he went away and prayed again for the third time, saying the same thing.

**45** Then he came back to the disciples and told them, "You might as well keep on sleeping and resting. <sup>&#123;Or Are you still sleeping and resting?&#125;</sup> Look! The time is near for the Son of Man to be betrayed into the hands of sinners.

**46** Get up! Let's go! See, the one who is betraying me is near!"

**47** <sup>&#123;Jesus is Arrested&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 14:43-50; Luke 22:47-53; John 18:3-12)&#125;</sup> d Just then, while Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. he&#125;</sup> was still speaking, Judas, one of the Twelve, arrived. A large crowd armed with swords and clubs was with him. They were from the high priests and elders of the people.

**48** Now the betrayer personally had given them a signal, saying, "The one I kiss <sup>&#123;People customarily greeted their friends with a kiss.&#125;</sup> is the man. Arrest him."

**49** So Judas <sup>&#123;Lit. he&#125;</sup> immediately went up to Jesus and said, "Hello, Rabbi!" <sup>&#123;Rabbi is Heb. for Master and/or Teacher .&#125;</sup> and kissed him tenderly.

**50** Jesus asked him, "Friend, why are you here?" <sup>&#123;Or do what you came for&#125;</sup> Then the other men <sup>&#123;Lit. they&#125;</sup> surged forward, took hold of Jesus, and arrested him.

**51** Suddenly, one of the men with Jesus reached out his hand, drew his sword, and struck the high priest's servant, cutting off his ear.

**52** Jesus told him, "Put your sword back in its place! Everyone who uses a sword will be killed by a sword.

**53** Don't you think that I could call on my Father, and he would send me more than twelve legions of angels now?

**54** How, then, would the Scriptures be fulfilled that say this must happen?"

**55** At this point, <sup>&#123;Lit. In that hour&#125;</sup> Jesus asked the crowds, "Have you come out with swords and clubs to arrest me as if I were a bandit? <sup>&#123;Or revolutionary&#125;</sup> Day after day I sat teaching in the Temple, yet you didn't arrest me.

**56** But all of this has happened so that the writings of the prophets might be fulfilled." Then all the disciples deserted Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. him&#125;</sup> and ran away.

**57** <sup>&#123;Jesus is Tried before the High Priest&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 14:53-65; Luke 22:54-55, 63-71; John 18:13-14, 19-24)&#125;</sup> d Those who had arrested Jesus took him to Caiaphas, the high priest, where the scribes and the elders had assembled.

**58** Peter, however, followed him at a distance as far as the high priest's courtyard. He went inside and sat down with the servants to see how this would end.

**59** Meanwhile, the high priests and the whole Council <sup>&#123;Or Sanhedrin&#125;</sup> were looking for false testimony against Jesus in order to have him put to death.

**60** But they couldn't find any, even though many false witnesses had come forward. At last two men came forward

**61** and stated, "This man said, «I can destroy the sanctuary of God and rebuild it in three days.»"

**62** At this, the high priest stood up and asked Jesus, <sup>&#123;Lit. him&#125;</sup> "Don't you have any answer to what these men are testifying against you?"

**63** But Jesus was silent. Then the high priest told him, "I command you by the living God to tell us if you are the Messiah, <sup>&#123;Or Christ&#125;</sup> the Son of God!"

**64** Jesus told him, "You have said so. Nevertheless I tell you, from now on you will see «the Son of Man seated at the right hand of Power'<sup>&#123;Ps 110:1&#125;</sup> and «coming on the clouds of heaven.»" <sup>&#123;Dan 7:13&#125;</sup>

**65** Then the high priest tore his robes and said, "He has blasphemed! Why do we still need witnesses? Listen! You yourselves have just heard the blasphemy!

**66** What is your verdict?" They replied, "He deserves to die!"

**67** Then they spit in his face and hit him. Some slapped him,

**68** saying, "Prophesy to us, you Messiah! <sup>&#123;Or Christ&#125;</sup> Who hit <sup>&#123;Lit. Who is the one who hit&#125;</sup> you?"

**69** <sup>&#123;Peter Denies Jesus&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 14:66-72; Luke 22:56-62; John 18:15-18, 25-27)&#125;</sup> d Now Peter was sitting outside in the courtyard when a servant girl came up to him and said, "You, too, were with Jesus the Galilean."

**70** But he denied it in front of them all, saying, "I don't know what you're talking about."

**71** As he went out to the gateway, another woman saw him and told those who were there, "This man was with Jesus from Nazareth." <sup>&#123;Or Jesus the Nazarene ; the Gk. Nazoraios may be a word play between Heb. netser, meaning branch (see Isa 11:1), and the name Nazareth.&#125;</sup>

**72** Again he denied it and swore with an oath, "I don't know the man!"

**73** After a little while, the people who were standing there came up and told Peter, "Obviously you're also one of them, because your accent gives you away."

**74** Then he began to curse violently. "I don't know the man!" he swore solemnly. Just then a rooster crowed.

**75** Peter remembered the words of Jesus when he said, "Before a rooster crows, you will deny me three times." Then he went outside and cried bitterly.

## Chapter 27

**1** <sup>&#123;Jesus is Taken to Pilate&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 15:1; Luke 23:1-2; John 18:28-32)&#125;</sup> d When morning came, all the high priests and elders of the people conspired against Jesus to put him to death.

**2** They bound him with chains, led him away, and handed him over to Pontius <sup>&#123;Other mss. lack Pontius&#125;</sup> Pilate, the governor.

**3** <sup>&#123;The Death of Judas&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Acts 1:18-19)&#125;</sup> d Then Judas, who had betrayed him, regretted what had happened when he saw that Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. he&#125;</sup> was condemned. He brought the 30 pieces of silver back to the high priests and elders,

**4** saying, "I have sinned by betraying innocent <sup>&#123;Other mss. read righteous&#125;</sup> blood." But they replied, "What do we care? Attend to that yourself."

**5** Then he flung the pieces of silver into the sanctuary and went outside. Then he went away and hanged himself.

**6** The high priests picked up the pieces of silver and said, "It is not lawful to put this into the Temple treasury, because it is blood money."

**7** So they decided to use the money to buy the Potter's Field as a burial ground for foreigners.

**8** That is why that field has been called the Field of Blood to this day.

**9** Then what had been declared through the prophet Jeremiah was fulfilled when he said, "They <sup>&#123;Or I&#125;</sup> took the 30 pieces of silver, the value of the man on whom a price had been set by the Israelis,

**10** and they <sup>&#123;Other mss. read I&#125;</sup> gave them for the potter's field, as the Lord commanded me." <sup>&#123;Zech 11:12-13; Jer 32:6-9&#125;</sup>

**11** <sup>&#123;Pilate Questions Jesus&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 15:2-5; Luke 23:3-5; John 18:33-38)&#125;</sup> d Meanwhile, Jesus was made to stand in front of the governor. The governor asked him, "Are you the king of the Jews?" Jesus said, "You say so."

**12** While Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. he&#125;</sup> was being accused by the high priests and elders, he made no reply.

**13** Then Pilate asked him, "Don't you hear how many charges they're bringing against you?"

**14** But Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. he&#125;</sup> did not reply at all, so that the governor was very surprised.

**15** <sup>&#123;Jesus is Sentenced to Death&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 15:6-15; Luke 23:13-25; John 18:39-19:16)&#125;</sup> d At every festival <sup>&#123;I.e. Passover Festival&#125;</sup> the governor had a custom of releasing to the crowd any prisoner whom they wanted.

**16** At that time they were holding a notorious prisoner named Barabbas. <sup>&#123;Other mss. read Jesus Barabbas&#125;</sup>

**17** So when the people <sup>&#123;Lit. they&#125;</sup> had gathered, Pilate asked them, "Which man do you want me to release for you Barabbas, <sup>&#123;Other mss. read Jesus Barabbas&#125;</sup> or Jesus who is called the Messiah?" <sup>&#123;Or Christ&#125;</sup>

**18** He did this <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks He did this&#125;</sup> because he knew that they had handed him over out of jealousy.

**19** While he was sitting on the judge's seat, his wife sent him a message. <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks a message&#125;</sup> It said, "Have nothing to do with that righteous man, because today I have suffered terribly due to a dream I had about him."

**20** But the high priests and elders persuaded the crowds to ask for Barabbas and to demand that Jesus be put to death.

**21** So the governor asked them, "Which of the two men do you want me to release for you?" "Barabbas!" they replied.

**22** Pilate asked them, "Then what should I do with Jesus, who is called the Messiah?" <sup>&#123;Or Christ&#125;</sup> They all said, "Let him be crucified!"

**23** He asked, "What has he done wrong?" But they kept shouting louder and louder, "Let him be crucified!"

**24** Pilate saw that he was getting nowhere, but that a riot was about to break out instead. So he took some water and washed his hands in front of the crowd, saying, "I am innocent of this man's <sup>&#123;Other mss. read this righteous man's&#125;</sup> blood. Attend to that yourselves."

**25** All the people answered, "Let his blood be on us and our children!"

**26** Then he released Barabbas for them, but he had Jesus whipped and handed over to be crucified.

**27** <sup>&#123;The Soldiers Make Fun of Jesus&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 15:16-20; John 19:2-3)&#125;</sup> d Then the governor's soldiers took Jesus into the imperial headquarters <sup>&#123;Lit. praetorium&#125;</sup> and gathered the whole company of soldiers around him.

**28** They stripped <sup>&#123;Other mss. read clothed&#125;</sup> him and put a scarlet robe on him.

**29** Twisting some thorns into a victor's crown, they placed it on his head and put <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks put&#125;</sup> a stick in his right hand. They knelt down in front of him and began making fun of him, saying, "Long live the king of the Jews!"

**30** Then they spit on him and took the stick and hit him repeatedly on his head.

**31** When they had finished making fun of him, they stripped him of the robe, put his own clothes back on him, and led him away to crucify him.

**32** <sup>&#123;Jesus is Crucified&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 15:21-32; Luke 23:26-43; John 19:17-27)&#125;</sup> d As they were leaving, they found a man from Cyrene named Simon, whom they forced to carry Jesus'<sup>&#123;Lit. his&#125;</sup> cross.

**33** When they came to a place called Golgotha (which means "Skull Place" ),

**34** they offered him a drink of wine mixed with gall. But when he tasted it, he refused to drink it.

**35** After they had crucified him, they determined who would get his clothes by throwing dice for them. <sup>&#123;The Gk. lacks for them&#125;</sup>

**36** Then they sat down there and continued guarding him.

**37** Above his head they placed the charge against him. It read, "This is Jesus, the king of the Jews."

**38** At that time two bandits <sup>&#123;Or revolutionaries&#125;</sup> were crucified with him, one on his right and the other on his left.

**39** Those who passed by kept insulting <sup>&#123;Or blaspheming&#125;</sup> him, shaking their heads,

**40** and saying, "You who were going to destroy the sanctuary and rebuild it in three days save yourself! If you are the Son of God, come down from the cross!"

**41** In the same way the high priests, along with the scribes and elders, were also making fun of him. They kept saying,

**42** "He saved others but can't save himself! He is the king of Israel. Let him <sup>&#123;Other mss. read If he is the king of Israel, let him&#125;</sup> come down from the cross now, and we will believe in him.

**43** He trusts in God. Let God <sup>&#123;Lit. him&#125;</sup> rescue him, if he wants to do so now. After all, he said, «I am the Son of God.» "

**44** In a similar way, the bandits <sup>&#123;Or revolutionaries&#125;</sup> who were being crucified with him kept insulting him.

**45** <sup>&#123;Jesus Dies on the Cross&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 15:33-41; Luke 23:44-49; John 19:28-30)&#125;</sup> d From noon <sup>&#123;Lit. the sixth hour&#125;</sup> on, darkness came over the whole land <sup>&#123;Or earth&#125;</sup> until three in the afternoon. <sup>&#123;Lit. the ninth hour&#125;</sup>

**46** About three o'clock, <sup>&#123;Lit. the ninth hour&#125;</sup> Jesus cried out with a loud voice, "Eli, eli, <sup>&#123;Eli, eli are Gk. transliterations for the Heb. My God, my God in Ps 22:1&#125;</sup> lema sabachthani?" , <sup>&#123;lema sabachthani is a Gk. transliteration for the Aram. rendering of the Heb. in Ps 22:1, which means Why have you forsaken me?&#125;</sup> which means, "My God, my God, why have you forsaken me?" <sup>&#123;Ps 22:1&#125;</sup>

**47** When some of the people standing there heard this, they said, "He's calling for Elijah." <sup>&#123;Elijah in Heb. sounds like Eli&#125;</sup>

**48** So one of the men ran off at once, took a sponge, and soaked it in some sour wine. Then he put it on a stick and offered Jesus <sup>&#123;Lit. him&#125;</sup> a drink.

**49** But the others kept saying, "Wait! Let's see if Elijah will come and save him." <sup>&#123;Other mss. read save him." And another took a spear and pierced his side, and water and blood came out.&#125;</sup>

**50** Then Jesus cried out with a loud voice again and died. <sup>&#123;Or and gave up his spirit&#125;</sup>

**51** Suddenly, the curtain <sup>&#123;This curtain separated the Holy Place from the Most Holy Place.&#125;</sup> in the sanctuary was torn in two from top to bottom, the earth shook, rocks were split open,

**52** tombs were opened, and many <sup>&#123;Lit. and the corpses of many&#125;</sup> saints who had died <sup>&#123;Lit. fallen asleep&#125;</sup> were brought back to life.

**53** After his resurrection, they came out of their tombs and went into the Holy City <sup>&#123;I.e. Jerusalem&#125;</sup> and appeared to many people.

**54** When the centurion <sup>&#123;A Roman centurion commanded about 100 men.&#125;</sup> and those guarding Jesus with him saw the earthquake and the other things that were taking place, they were terrified and said, "This man certainly was the Son of God!"

**55** Now many women were also there, watching from a distance. They had accompanied Jesus from Galilee and had ministered to <sup>&#123;Or provided for&#125;</sup> him.

**56** Among them were Mary Magdalene, <sup>&#123;Or Mary of Magdala&#125;</sup> Mary the mother of James and Joseph, and the mother of Zebedee's sons.

**57** <sup>&#123;Jesus is Buried&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 15:42-47; Luke 23:50-56; John 19:38-42)&#125;</sup> d Later that evening, a rich man arrived from Arimathea. His name was Joseph, and he had become a disciple of Jesus.

**58** He went to Pilate and asked for the body of Jesus, and Pilate ordered it to be done.

**59** So Joseph took the body and wrapped it in a clean linen cloth.

**60** Then he placed it in his own new tomb, which he had cut out of the rock. After rolling a large stone across the door of the tomb, he left,

**61** but Mary Magdalene <sup>&#123;Or Mary of Magdala&#125;</sup> and the other Mary remained there, sitting in front of the tomb.

**62** <sup>&#123;The Tomb is Secured&#125;</sup> d The following day (that is, after the Day of Preparation), the high priests and Pharisees gathered before Pilate

**63** and said, "Sir, we remember how that impostor said while he was still alive, «After three days I will be raised.»

**64** Therefore, order the tomb to be secured until the third day, or his disciples may go and steal him and then tell the people, «He has been raised from the dead.'Then the last deception would be worse than the first one."

**65** Pilate told them, "You have <sup>&#123;Or Take&#125;</sup> a military guard. Go and make the tomb <sup>&#123;Lit. it&#125;</sup> as secure as you know how."

**66** So they went and secured the tomb by putting a seal on the stone in the presence of the guards.

## Chapter 28

**1** <sup>&#123;Jesus is Raised from the Dead&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 16:1-8; Luke 24:1-12; John 20:1-10)&#125;</sup> d After the Sabbaths, <sup>&#123;I.e., the Passover and Saturday&#125;</sup> around dawn on the first day of the week, <sup>&#123;Lit. first of the Sabbaths&#125;</sup> Mary Magdalene <sup>&#123;Or Mary of Magdala&#125;</sup> and the other Mary went to take a look at the burial site. <sup>&#123;Or the tomb&#125;</sup>

**2** Suddenly, there was a powerful earthquake, because an angel of the Lord had come down from heaven, approached the stone, rolled it away, and was sitting on top of it.

**3** His appearance was bright as lightning, and his clothes were white as snow.

**4** Trembling from fear, even the guards themselves became catatonic. <sup>&#123;Lit. became like the dead&#125;</sup>

**5** Responding to the women, the angel said, "Stop being frightened! I know you're looking for Jesus, who was crucified.

**6** He is not here. He has been raised, just as he said. Come and see the place where he <sup>&#123;Other mss. read the Lord&#125;</sup> was lying.

**7** Then go quickly and tell his disciples that he is risen from the dead. He is going ahead of you into Galilee, and you will see him there. Remember, I have told you!"

**8** So they quickly left the tomb, terrified but also ecstatic, and ran to tell Jesus'<sup>&#123;Lit. his&#125;</sup> disciples.

**9** Suddenly, Jesus met them and said, "Greetings!" They went up to him, took hold of his feet, and worshipped him.

**10** Then Jesus told them, "Stop being frightened! Go and tell my brothers to leave for Galilee, and they will see me there."

**11** <sup>&#123;The Guards Report to the High Priests&#125;</sup> d While the women were on their way, some of the guards went into the city and told the high priests everything that had happened.

**12** So they met with the elders and agreed on a plan to give the soldiers a large <sup>&#123;Or a sufficient&#125;</sup> amount of money.

**13** They said, "Say that Jesus'<sup>&#123;Lit. his&#125;</sup> disciples came at night and stole him while you were sleeping.

**14** If this is reported to the governor, we'll personally persuade him to keep you out of trouble." <sup>&#123;Lit. from worry&#125;</sup>

**15** So the soldiers <sup>&#123;Lit. they&#125;</sup> took the money, did as they were instructed, and this story has been spread among the Jews to this day.

**16** <sup>&#123;Jesus Commissions His Disciples&#125;</sup> <sup>&#123;(Mark 16:14-18; Luke 24:36-49; John 20:19-23; Acts 1:6-8)&#125;</sup> d The eleven disciples went into Galilee to the hillside to which Jesus had directed them.

**17** When they saw him, they worshipped him, though some had doubts.

**18** Then Jesus came up and told them, "All authority in heaven and on earth has been given to me.

**19** Therefore, as you go, disciple people in all nations, baptizing them in the name of the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit,

**20** teaching them to obey everything that I've commanded you. And remember, I am with you each and every day  <sup>&#123;Lit. you all the days&#125;</sup> until the end of the age." <sup>&#123;Other mss. read age. Amen&#125;</sup>

---

# The Ethos of Jesus

This section reconstructs the governing whole taught and enacted by Jesus during his life: its public declarations, interaction rules, membership, allocation of responsibility and resources, treatment of other ethoses, standards of proof, and forms of authority. It uses all four Gospels as witnesses without pretending that they are one report. A claim enters the reconstruction only with its witnesses and status attached.

“The ethos of Jesus” is narrower than “the ethos of Christ.” The former concerns what the Gospel narratives attribute to Jesus' earthly teaching and action. Instructions placed after the resurrection may testify to intended continuity, but their communal enactment belongs to the post-death ethos of Christ. This boundary prevents later apostolic and Pauline structures from being read backward as if Jesus had already stated them.

The present reconstruction is provisional. Its strongest initial findings concern allegiance, enacted proof, positive reciprocity, responsibility for representatives, challenge procedures, missionary dependence, servant authority, treatment of opponents, and the conditions for joining or leaving.

---

# Reconstructed Ethos

This is the synthesis layer. It assembles only claims established in the detailed studies and keeps their evidential status visible. It does not turn every instruction into an ethos declaration: a declaration must contribute to the governing whole by specifying allegiance, interaction, membership, representation, judgment, resources, responsibility, or change.

The reconstruction presently indicates a public and enacted ethos under one ultimate master; positive initiative toward others; costly but voluntary discipleship; delegated mission; non-dominating authority; behavioral tests for representatives; correction with escalation and witnesses; and active treatment of neighbors, outsiders, and enemies. Whether these parts constitute a complete, internally consistent ethos remains an audit question.

---

# Declarations

The current reconstruction contains the following high-level declarations. These are compact names for conclusions argued elsewhere, not replacements for their textual witnesses.

| Declaration | Current formulation | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `one_master` | Ultimate allegiance cannot be divided between incompatible ruling ethoses. | explicit |
| `constitutive_priority` | Love of God and neighbor governs subordinate requirements. | explicit |
| `positive_reciprocity` | Initiate toward others the treatment one would properly seek from them. | explicit core; qualification contested |
| `proof_by_fruits` | Representatives are evaluated by enacted results, not profession alone. | explicit |
| `enemy_positive_action` | Opposition does not cancel the duty to act positively toward the opponent. | explicit |
| `servant_governance` | Authority among disciples is proved by service rather than domination. | explicit |
| `authorized_representation` | Receiving or rejecting an authorized messenger reaches the sender represented. | explicit |
| `graduated_correction` | An alleged wrong is addressed privately, then with witnesses, then by the assembly. | explicit in Matthew |

The table is not yet a complete constitution. Jesus does not leave one document that names all offices, proof standards, membership conditions, amendment rules, or remedies. Those elements must be reconstructed with their scope limitations visible.

---

# Interaction Rules

Jesus' ethos is most visible where one actor's conduct changes another actor's ethical position. Its main reconstructed interactions presently include:

- member → neighbor or enemy: initiate beneficial treatment without waiting for reciprocity;
- sender → representative: authorize a limited mission and remain implicated in its reception;
- representative → household: offer a message and peace, accept hospitality, and withdraw after refusal rather than compel entry;
- member → member who wrongs: seek repair privately before widening judgment;
- assembly → disputed member: hear witnesses, decide compatibility, and change the relationship if correction fails;
- leader → members: model service and carry the burdens created by authority;
- audience → claimant: test profession by conduct and results.

These rules vary in addressee and institutional scale. A command to an individual disciple cannot automatically be imposed on a state, and a protocol given to a gathered community cannot automatically be reduced to private feeling. The detailed pages identify the level at which each rule operates.

---

# Membership Rules

Membership in Jesus' circle is joinable but costly. Calls to follow are invitations that reorganize work, property, family priority, safety, and allegiance; sayings about calculating a building or a campaign require prospective members to see the cost before commitment. The narratives also show refusals and departures without a procedure for physical compulsion.

The evidence does not support one simple initiation rule for Jesus' lifetime. The Twelve are appointed, other disciples follow, households support the mission, some recipients are healed without joining, and unaffiliated actors can be treated as compatible. Baptism appears in the wider Gospel setting, but the stable baptismal membership of communities acting after Jesus' death belongs to the ethos of Christ.

**Current finding — `strongly implied`:** Jesus' ethos is voluntarily joinable, demands disclosed priority over competing allegiances, and permits factual departure. Whether threats of eschatological loss make it fully “non-binding” under the Theory of Ethos remains contested.

---

# Roles and Authority

Jesus is teacher, sender, judge, healer, and leader within the Gospel narratives. The Twelve and other disciples become learners, messengers, witnesses, and temporary representatives; householders become hosts; crowds remain audiences rather than members merely by listening. A role is established by an assignment and a relation, not by an honorific alone.

Delegated authority is real but bounded. The mission discourses specify message, conduct, dependence, and response to refusal. Reception of a messenger can count as reception of the sender, yet warnings about false prophets and fruit prevent the mere use of Jesus' name from proving authorization. Among disciples, greatness is measured by service, which changes the obligations and proof of rule without eliminating every difference of role.

**Current finding — `strongly implied`:** authority in Jesus' ethos is personal, delegated for defined work, answerable through outcomes, and normatively non-dominating. A complete appointment and removal procedure is not supplied during Jesus' life.

---

# Present Audit

At this stage, the reconstruction has substantial evidence for a minimal identity, missionary responsibility, awareness of mission resources, challenge through questions and correction, partial cooperation with outsiders, acceptance of witnesses, behavioral proof, delegated representation, and servant governance.

Important gaps remain. The Gospels do not present a single public register of declarations; the route for amending the whole ethos is unclear; procedures differ among witnesses; reparations are commanded more clearly than institutional liability is assigned; and post-death baptism, offices, and inter-community government must not be imported into Jesus' lifetime. Apparent tensions concerning outsiders, publicity, family, law, judgment, and departure are still open.

The proper status is therefore **provisional reconstruction**, not “ethically complete ethos.” Completeness can be claimed only after the phylogeny matrix and contradiction audit identify the evidence for every required attribute and answer the strongest counterexamples.

---

# Minimal and Meta-Ethos

These studies ask whether Jesus' teaching meets the minimum conditions of an identifiable ethos and how it regulates encounters with another ethos. The relevant evidence concerns unified allegiance, public enactment, reciprocal initiative, recognition of outsiders, and the handling of apparent contradictions. The goal is not to retrofit the Theory of Ethos vocabulary into every saying, but to test whether the textual rules perform equivalent work.

---

# One Ethos and One Master

**Proposed declaration — `explicit`**

> `one_master`: A person cannot sustainably enact two incompatible ruling allegiances; service to God displaces wealth as master.

The direct witnesses are [Matthew 6:24](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Matthew%206%3A24&version=ISV) and [Luke 16:13](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Luke%2016%3A13&version=ISV). In both, the conflict is not possession versus poverty in the abstract but service to two masters whose demands divide loyalty. [Mark 3:24–27](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Mark%203%3A24-27&version=ISV) supplies a related institutional test: a kingdom or household divided against itself cannot remain standing.

The ethical actors are the disciple, God as the claimed ultimate authority, and wealth personified as a rival master. The rule allows subordinate commitments only while they remain compatible with the ruling allegiance. Family, state, occupation, or property therefore require separate compatibility tests; the saying does not prove that every secondary ethos must disappear.

**Theory mapping:** Minimal Ethos (`one_ethos`), consistency, person–institution relation. The text establishes undivided mastery more directly than it establishes the theory's stronger requirement that the complete ethos be published in one place.

**Secondary virtue note:** temperance may help resist wealth's rule, but allegiance—not self-control—is the constitutional issue.

---

# Public and Enacted Ethos

**Proposed declaration — `strongly implied`**

> `public_without_performance`: The ethos must become knowable through enacted results, while acts directed toward God or a recipient must not be converted into displays for status.

[Matthew 5:14–16](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Matthew%205%3A14-16&version=ISV) expects visible good works whose result directs recognition toward God. [Matthew 6:1–18](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Matthew%206%3A1-18&version=ISV) warns against performing almsgiving, prayer, and fasting in order to be seen. The two passages distinguish availability of proof from reputation-seeking publication: an ethos can be publicly identifiable through its effects while protecting the recipient and refusing theatrical self-advertisement.

This does not yet amount to the Theory of Ethos declaration `live_ethos`, which requires the believer's whole governing ethos to be shared. The Gospel evidence is distributed across speech, action, disciples, and narrative rather than supplied as a complete public constitution.

**Challenge:** observers need enough evidence to test a representative, but secrecy can make a particular act unverifiable. The reconstruction still needs a protocol for proof that preserves the privacy and dignity of those receiving help.

**Secondary virtue note:** humility helps explain the refusal of status-seeking, but the primary question is how an ethos proves itself without exploiting its audience.

---

# Ethical Economy

## Witness

appears in:

- [Matthew 20:1-16](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Matthew%2020%3A%201-16&version=ISV)

### Quotes

[Matthew 20:1-16](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Matthew%2020%3A%201-16&version=ISV) `The Workers in the Vineyard`

20 “The kingdom from heaven is like a landowner who went out early in the morning to hire workers for his vineyard. 2 After agreeing to pay the workers one denarius a day, he sent them into his vineyard. 3 When he went out about nine o’clock, he saw others standing in the marketplace without work. 4 He told them, ‘You go into the vineyard, too, and I will pay you whatever is right.’ 5 So off they went. He went out again about noon and about three o’clock and did the same thing. 6 About five o’clock he went out and found some others standing around. He asked them, ‘Why are you standing here all day long without work?’ 7 They told him, ‘Because no one has hired us.’ He told them, ‘You go into the vineyard as well.’

8 “When evening came, the owner of the vineyard told his manager, ‘Call the workers and give them their wages, beginning with the last and ending with the first.’ 9 Those who were hired at five o’clock came, and each received a denarius.

10 “When the first came, they thought they would receive more, but each received a denarius as well. 11 When they received it, they began to complain to the landowner, 12 ‘These last fellows worked only one hour, but you paid them the same as us, and we’ve been working all day, enduring the scorching heat!’

13 “But he told one of them, ‘Friend, I’m not treating you unfairly. You did agree with me for a denarius, didn’t you? 14 Take what is yours and go. I want to give this last man as much as I gave you. 15 I am allowed to do what I want with my own money, am I not? Or are you envious because I’m generous?’

16 “In the same way, the last will be first, and the first will be last, because many are called, but few are chosen.”

## Consequential Details

- the workers were waiting for work when they were given the invitation
- a denarius was the correct amount for a day's work
- the landowner intended to teach a lesson from the situation: ‘Call the workers and give them their wages, beginning with the last and ending with the first.’

## Ethical Lessons

- an ethos does its own accounting
- the members of an ethos should accept the results of internal accounting if the results are just
- the results do not need to seem fair: only just
- an ethos has the freedom to pay forward. Forward payment may not be fair, but it may remain just
- an ethos performs its own judgement of value
- the judgement of an ethos has to be honored

## Indirect Ethical Lessons

- an ethos is treating everybody fairly in aggregate: the ethos is timeless and anybody less than timeless cannot be as fair as something timeless

# Ethical Generosity

## Witness

appears in:

- [Matthew 5:39](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Matthew%205%3A39%20&version=ISV)
- [Luke 6:29](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Luke%206%3A29&version=ISV)

### Quotes

[Matthew 5:39](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Matthew%205%3A39%20&version=ISV) `The Sermon on the Mount`

39 But I tell you not to resist an evildoer. On the contrary, whoever slaps you on the right cheek, turn the other to him as well.

[Luke 6:29](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Luke%206%3A29&version=ISV) `The Sermon on the Plain`

29 If someone strikes you on the cheek, offer him the other one as well, and if someone takes your coat, don’t keep back your shirt, either.

## Consequential Details

- action is demanded, not passive acceptance

## Ethical Lessons

- ethical generosity is asted while inhabiting an ethos, not as an individual
- this has ethical effect especially if the first actor (the abuser) is inhabiting an ethos too

## Indirect Ethical Lessons

- this would work best if witnesses present or a record is created
- an ethos has the duty to record such events

# Ethical Identity

## Witness

appears in:

- [John 8:1-11](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=John%208%3A1-11&version=ISV) `The Woman Caught in Adultery`

### Quotes

[John 8:1-11](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=John%208%3A1-11&version=ISV) `The Woman Caught in Adultery`

1 Jesus, however, went to the Mount of Olives. 2 At daybreak he appeared again in the Temple, and all the people came to him. So he sat down and began to teach them. 3 But the scribes and the Pharisees brought a woman who had been caught in adultery. After setting her before them, 4 they told him, “Teacher, this woman has been caught in the very act of adultery. 5 Now in the Law, Moses commanded us to stone such women to death. What do you say?” 6 They said this to test him, so that they might have a charge against him. But Jesus bent down and began to write on the ground with his finger.

7 When they persisted in questioning him, he straightened up and told them, “Let the person among you who is without sin be the first to throw a stone at her.” 8 Then he bent down again and continued writing on the ground. 9 When they heard this, they went away one by one, beginning with the oldest, and he was left alone with the woman standing there. 10 Then Jesus stood up and asked her, “Dear lady, where are your accusers? Hasn’t anyone condemned you?”

11 “No one, sir,” she replied.

Then Jesus said, “I don’t condemn you, either. Go home, and from now on don’t sin anymore.”

## Consequential Details

- the woman was brought alone to be stoned to death
- the Law of Moses required that both parties caught in adultery be put to death (especially the man).
  - [Leviticus 20:10](https://www.sefaria.org/Leviticus.20.10?lang=bi&with=all&lang2=en)
    > If a man commits adultery with a married woman—committing adultery with another man’s wife—the adulterer and the adulteress shall be put to death.
  - [Deuteronomy 22:22](https://www.sefaria.org/Deuteronomy.22.22?lang=bi&with=all&lang2=en)
    > If a man is found lying with another man’s wife, both of them—the man and the woman with whom he lay—shall die. Thus you will sweep away evil from Israel.
- Jesus is writing with his finger in the dust. Early Church Fathers like St. Jerome say that Jesus was writing the names of the accusers.
  - [Jeremiah 17:13](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Jeremiah%2017%3A13&version=ISV)
    > Lord, you are the hope of Israel;
 all who forsake you will be put to shame.
 Those who turn aside from you will be
 written in the dust,
 because they have forsaken the Lord,
 the spring of living water.
- the accusers renounced the judgement one by one - beginning with the most learned

## Ethical Lessons

- the identity of each accuser should be known before an ethical judgement begins
- an ethical trial can be annulled due to a vicious procedure

## Indirect Ethical Lessons

- ethos building at scale needs identity management solutions

# Ethical Unity

## Witness

appears in:

- [Matthew 23:1-12](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Matthew%2023%3A1-12&version=ISV) `Jesus Denounces the Scribes and the Pharisees`

### Quotes

[Matthew 23:1-12](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Matthew%2023%3A1-12&version=ISV) `Jesus Denounces the Scribes and the Pharisees`

1 Then Jesus told the crowds and his disciples, 2 “The scribes and the Pharisees administer the authority of Moses, 3 so do whatever they tell you and follow it, but stop doing what they do, because they don’t do what they say. 4 They tie up burdens that are heavy and unbearable and lay them on people’s shoulders, but they refuse to lift a finger to remove them.

5 “They do everything to be seen by people. They increase the size of their phylacteries and lengthen the tassels of their garments. 6 They love to have the places of honor at festivals, the best seats in the synagogues, 7 to be greeted in the marketplaces, and to be called ‘Rabbi’ by people.

8 “But you are not to be called ‘Rabbi,’ because you have only one teacher, and all of you are brothers. 9 And don’t call anyone on earth ‘Father,’ because you have only one Father, the one in heaven. 10 Nor are you to be called ‘Teachers,’ because you have only one teacher, the Messiah! 11 The person who is greatest among you must be your servant. 12 Whoever exalts himself will be humbled, and whoever humbles himself will be exalted.

## Consequential Details

- the Greek word used for 'Messiah' is The Christ / ὁ Χριστός
- Jesus does not refer to his ethos here, but to the ethos of Christ
- the word for 'Teachers' also means guides, leaders, instructors

## Ethical Lessons

- the ethos is the one who exalts or sets its hierarchy
- if somebody exalts himself: he does so without an ethos
- the one who humbles himself towards the ethos - will be exalted
- do not accept personal exaltation
- the work done in the name of the ethos has the merit attributed to the ethos
- the ethos members are brothers from the ethical point of view
- the ethos is impersonal
- the ethos is trascendental and unique

## Indirect Ethical Lessons

- somebody exalted by ethos has the duty to accept the exaltation in the name of the ethos

# Ethical Wealth

## Witness

Appears in:

- [Matthew 19:23-30](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Matthew%2019%3A23-30&version=ISV) `Salvation and Reward`
- [Mark 10:23-31](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Mark%2010%3A23-31&version=ISV) `Salvation and Reward`
- [Luke 18:24-30](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Luke%2018%3A24-30&version=ISV) `Salvation and Reward`

### Quotes

[Matthew 19:23-30](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Matthew%2019%3A23-30&version=ISV) `Salvation and Reward`

23 Then Jesus told his disciples, “I tell all of you with certainty, it will be hard for a rich person to get into the kingdom from heaven. 24 Again I tell you, it is easier for a camel to squeeze through the eye of a needle than for a rich person to get into the kingdom of God.”

25 When the disciples heard this, they were completely astonished. “Who, then, can be saved?” they asked.

26 Jesus looked at them intently and said, “For humans this is impossible, but for God all things are possible.”

27 “Look!” Peter replied. “We have left everything and followed you. So what will we get?”

28 Jesus told them, “I tell all of you with certainty, when the Son of Man sits on his glorious throne in the renewed creation, you who have followed me will also sit on twelve thrones, governing the twelve tribes of Israel. 29 In fact, everyone who has left his homes, brothers, sisters, father, mother, children, or fields because of my name will receive a hundred times as much and will inherit eternal life. 30 But many who are first will be last, and the last will be first.”

[Mark 10:23-31](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Mark%2010%3A23-31&version=ISV) `Salvation and Reward`

23 Then Jesus looked around and told his disciples, “How hard it will be for those who are wealthy to enter the kingdom of God!” 24 The disciples were startled by these words, but Jesus told them again, “Children, how hard it is for those who trust in their wealth to get into the kingdom of God! 25 It’s easier for a camel to squeeze through the eye of a needle than for a rich person to get into the kingdom of God.”

26 The disciples were utterly amazed and asked one another, “Then who can be saved?”

27 Jesus looked at them intently and said, “For humans it’s impossible, but not for God. All things are possible for God.”

28 Then Peter began to say to him, “See, we have left everything and followed you.” 29 Jesus said, “I tell all of you with certainty, there is no one who has left his home, brothers, sisters, mother, father, children, or fields because of me and the gospel 30 who will not receive a hundred times as much here in this world—homes, brothers, sisters, mothers, children, and fields, along with persecution—as well as eternal life in the age to come. 31 But many who are first will be last, and the last will be first.”

[Luke 18:24-30](https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Luke%2018%3A24-30&version=ISV) `Salvation and Reward`

24 So when Jesus saw how sad he was, he said, “How hard it is for rich people to get into the kingdom of God! 25 Indeed, it’s easier for a camel to squeeze through the eye of a needle than for a rich person to get into the kingdom of God.”

26 Those who were listening to Jesus asked, “Then who can be saved?”

27 Jesus replied, “The things that are impossible for people are possible for God.”

28 Then Peter said, “See, we have left everything we have and followed you.”

29 Jesus told them, “I tell all of you with certainty, there is no one who has left his home, wife, brothers, parents, or children because of the kingdom of God 30 who will not receive many times as much in this world, as well as eternal life in the age to come.”

## Consequential Details

- the Mosaic Law had no problem admitting the rich as ethos members.
- the ethos of Jesus had a higher standard of entry

### Possibility

God knows all perspectives. Here are just 2 perspectives where what seemed impossible becomes possible:
![the written word](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ctzurcanu/ethical-christian/refs/heads/main/static/img/camilon.png)

1. The written Greek word "κάμηλον" - kamēlon - the camel passes through the eye of the needle.

2. The concept of "camel", the essence of all camels that exists in the Logos of God, is without spatial dimension. It can pass through any arbitrary region of space.

When something is impossible from the material point of view, it may be possible from the mental or conceptual point of view.

### Other Descriptions

#### Yoga Sūtras

In Yoga Sūtra 1.40, Patañjali writes:

परमाणुपरममहत्त्वान्तोऽस्य वशीकार

paramāṇuparamamahattvānto'sya vaśīkāraḥ

Paramanu (परमाणु) – “The smallest particle, an atom”
Paramamahattva (परममहत्त्व) – “The greatest, the vast universe”
Antah (अन्तः) – “Inside, within”
Asya (अस्य) – “His, of the yogi”
Vashikarah (वशीकरण) – “Control, mastery”

This spiritual attainment describes the perception of a concept/essence in the mind space buddhi of the meditation practitioner: the object becomes dimensionless or takes the whole universe.
When a camel is seen in that mental space, it is the essence of all camels and it becomes the concept that is atomic and isolated from other concepts. It becomes a Metaphysical object.

#### Modern AI

The latent space of any modern AI is a verifiable implementation of the idea of Platonic Forms where the essence (a concept) is understood as a vector with thousands of dimensions: it is a point without spatial dimension. Modern AI is a practical proof of the existence and mechanics of what was in the past considered Metaphysics.

### Additional Conditions

Since Jesus mentioned: "25 It’s easier for a camel to squeeze through the eye of a needle than for a rich person to get into the kingdom of God.", it follows that this is not the only condition for the rich to attain ethical wealth.
The other conditions are the ones that the particular ethos demands. For a good example: the wealth to be better administered in the name of the ethos by that particular person than by most or any other.

## Ethical Lessons

- each ethos comes with its own specific filter
- the ethos is timeless, and membership in it is timeless
- what seems impossible for a human is possible for an ethos
- the understanding of Metaphysics is required of any rich Christian
- it is impossible for a rich man who is motivated by materialism to be Christian
- being rich and making your wealth benefit the Christian ethos may be superior to detachment from desires, family, wealth
- it is possible to be rich and Christian if you are motivated by ethical generosity

## Indirect Ethical Lessons

### Application on Elon Musk

- he understands the details of Machine Learning; therefore, Metaphysics
- he created virtuous financial cycles
- made many people wealthy
- did not steal: the money was earned by means of honest transactions
- he is able to manage and re-invest better than governments and banks - much better than the average investor
- he invested in the preservation of free speech
- on top: he is exposing himself to public challenges, insults, bad blood
